# Decisions Log

Every game-direction decision, newest at the bottom. Add a row whenever something is decided (in a Claude thread or on your own) and link the [[Sessions/2026-10-17|session note]] where it happened. The [[Design/Game Design Document|Game Design Document]] should always match this log.

| Date | Decision | Why / notes | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-10-17 | Working title is **Sell Swords!** | "for now"; alternatives were Create Swords! and Craft Swords! | [[Sessions/2026-10-17]] |
| 2026-10-17 | **No timer** on anvil shaping; no forge/reheat step | Simpler; matches the pottery wheel | [[Sessions/2026-10-17]] |
| 2026-10-17 | **6 players per server, 6 plots**, one per player | | [[Sessions/2026-10-17]] |
| 2026-10-17 | Blades are **always recoverable** (undo/redo + free reset) | Over-hammering only costs time | [[Sessions/2026-10-17]] |
| 2026-10-17 | **Gems can be bought with Robux** | Everything stays earnable by playing (GDD §15) | [[Sessions/2026-10-17]] |
| 2026-10-17 | **No Rojo:** code lives in the Studio place, edited through the Studio MCP | Simpler setup; replaces the Rojo/GitHub item in the [[Development/Roadmap|Roadmap]] | [[Sessions/2026-10-17]] |
| 2026-10-17 | **Prototype and compare** the three new shaping concepts from [[Archive/Create Swords!|Create Swords!]] against the current silhouette anvil, as switchable modes; keep the winner after playtest | Current anvil stays as the baseline | [[Sessions/2026-10-17]] |
| 2026-10-17 | **Forge is just a station** (put ingot in, wait, take out), no skill | Supersedes "no forge/reheat step" for the new concepts | [[Sessions/2026-10-17]] |
| 2026-10-17 | **Forge resets the blade:** putting a sword back in the forge returns the metal to the rectangular blank | Keeps blades always recoverable | [[Sessions/2026-10-17]] |
| 2026-10-17 | **Concept 3 (osu points, 10 s):** hitting at least a minimum number of points produces a sword with a lower grade and an imperfect shape; you can always reheat to start over | Adds a timer for this mode only | [[Sessions/2026-10-17]] |
| 2026-10-17 | **Concept 2 (8 clicks):** shaping gives no quality; value comes only from material and finishes | | [[Sessions/2026-10-17]] |
| 2026-10-17 | **Concept 3 imperfect shape = "partly formed":** the blade morphs evenly from the rectangular blank toward the target; each hit moves it 1/12 of the way, so misses leave it too wide all over rather than wavy | Chosen over "wobbly all over" and "lumps where you missed" | [[Sessions/2026-10-17]] |
| 2026-10-17 | **Blade is drawn with an EditableMesh** (one smooth mesh, vertices moved after each hit) instead of stacked slices | Stacked slices looked jagged; wedge slices were the fallback. Check Roblox's EditableMesh rules for published games before launch | [[Sessions/2026-10-17]] |
| 2026-10-17 | **Accent lines appear automatically on the finished sword** (bevel lines on every blade; a fuller groove where the blueprint has one), no extra step | Chosen over fuller/etching skill steps; etching at the etching table stays in GDD §6.3 | [[Sessions/2026-10-17]] |
| 2026-10-17 | **Sharpened edges:** blades have a spine and bevels sloping to a thin edge; a light bevel while shaping, ground fully sharp on Finish | Partly answers the shaping-depth question (shaping stays 2D, the look is 3D) | [[Sessions/2026-10-17]] |
| 2026-10-17 | **Hilt Bench**: pick a carried blade + an unlocked hilt, attach (quick, no minigame); the sword is then complete and goes to the inventory as a held Tool | Display/selling to NPCs comes with the shop phase | [[Sessions/2026-10-17]] |
| 2026-10-17 | **Hilts give a % price bonus** (+0% to +40%) and **unlock by swords completed** until coins exist | Replaces the GDD's earlier "+flat value" | [[Sessions/2026-10-17]] |
| 2026-10-17 | **Finished blades go into a carried list** (no limit for now) | A limited blade rack was the alternative | [[Sessions/2026-10-17]] |
| 2026-10-17 | **Shop v1:** one shared 3-slot display rack; the sword's owner gets the offers; coins in leaderstats; Farmer / Adventurer (likes Shortswords) / Knight (likes Fine+ until Longswords exist) | Shared rack until the 6 player plots (Phase 2) | [[Sessions/2026-10-17]] |
| 2026-10-17 | **Blueprints can be traced from Creator Store swords** (option 2); bending store meshes (option 3) is blocked by Roblox for meshes we don't own | First one: Katana from "Sword katana" by Haantoca | [[Sessions/2026-10-17]] |
| 2026-10-17 | **Self-check: ★–★★ blueprints must be Masterwork-reachable by the bot, ★★★+ at least Superior** | Harder silhouettes are meant to be harder (GDD §6.2) | [[Sessions/2026-10-17]] |
| 2026-10-17 | **Core loop (Allen's version):** roll ingot → forge → anvil (pick ingot + blueprint) → Hilt Bench → inventory → sword holder (+ to pick a sword) → NPC sale → upgrades | Replaces the GDD §4 loop | [[Sessions/2026-10-17]] |
| 2026-10-17 | **Forge: drop in, collect later** (~15 s, upgradeable); heating comes before every sword | Supersedes "no forge step" for the main loop | [[Sessions/2026-10-17]] |
| 2026-10-17 | **Quench/temper step dropped**; the forge is the only timed step | Avoids two waits per sword | [[Sessions/2026-10-17]] |
| 2026-10-17 | **Seat the hilt**: three timed hammer taps on the pommel, small bonus for good timing | Quick interactive hilt step | [[Sessions/2026-10-17]] |
| 2026-10-17 | **Roblox's built-in backpack** for the toolbar/inventory for now | Custom 8-slot toolbar later if capacity upgrades are wanted | [[Sessions/2026-10-17]] |
| 2026-10-17 | **Holder + is a proximity prompt** ("+ Display a sword", E) rather than a floating clickable + | Native on keyboard, touch and gamepad, and testable; a clickable floating + can come back in the art pass | [[Sessions/2026-10-17]] |
| 2026-10-17 | **Forge and anvil open a window of your ingots** (forge: put in / take out with timers; anvil: choose a hot ingot), the same picker as the sword holders | Allen: selecting from a window instead of holding the item | [[Sessions/2026-10-17]] |
| 2026-10-17 | **Blades are 0.06 studs thick** (about 1/12 of the blank's width) | The held Dagger looked too thick | [[Sessions/2026-10-17]] |
| 2026-10-17 | **Held swords: upright, sharp edge leading**, flat side sideways | Matches a real katana grip; replaced a flat-forward grip | [[Sessions/2026-10-17]] |
| 2026-10-17 | **UI is designed in Figma first** ([[Art/UI Design|UI Design]]), starting with the forge window | Style follows the station-kit concept art; Luckiest Guy + Fredoka (both Roblox fonts) | [[Sessions/2026-10-17]] |
| 2026-10-17 | **Forge window is just your ingot cards**: tap one, the window closes and the forge starts heating it. No Put in button, no slots or timers in the window | Allen: the first Figma draft was over-engineered. Replaces "take it out from the same window" | [[Sessions/2026-10-17]] |
| 2026-10-17 | **Window titles name the action** ("PICK AN INGOT"), not the station ("FORGE") | Allen | [[Sessions/2026-10-17]] |
| 2026-10-17 | **Taking a hot ingot out:** a timer floats above the forge while it heats; when done, a native **Take out** (E) prompt appears on the forge | Allen. Chosen over "goes straight to your inventory" | [[Sessions/2026-10-17]] |
| 2026-10-17 | **Forge full:** if both slots are busy, E doesn't open the window; a quick "Forge is full" message shows | Allen. Chosen over greyed-out cards | [[Sessions/2026-10-17]] |
| 2026-10-17 | **One picker style** for every "put something into a station" window (forge, anvil, holders, Hilt Bench): icon + action title + close, row of cards, tap to pick | Allen | [[Sessions/2026-10-17]] |
| 2026-10-17 | **Sword holder and Hilt Bench use the picker style**: "PICK A SWORD" (price tag); Hilt Bench is two pickers in a row, "PICK A BLADE" (grade tag) then "PICK A HILT" (bonus tag) | Allen asked for the same style; showing only unlocked hilts is Claude's default, easy to change | [[Sessions/2026-10-17]] |
| 2026-10-17 | **Dark picker style after *Create Pottery!*'s "Select Wet Pottery" window**: translucent dark panel with dot texture, thin border, Montserrat Title Case titles ("Select Ingot"), ring close button; accents from rarity-glow cards and a title-bar shimmer; empty state with an orange hint | Allen: the first look was too basic and too bright. Replaces the chunky wood/parchment style and the "PICK AN INGOT" wording | [[Sessions/2026-10-17]] |
| 2026-10-18 | **Pickers follow [[Art/UI Concept - Furnace|UI Concept - Furnace]]**: warm chocolate window, station name + action subtitle ("Forge" / "Choose an ingot to heat."), big 3D item cards, stacked quantities, muted rarity chips, amber hover; after picking a toast "Ingot loaded" + "Iron · Heating" billboard. Locked hilts show dimmed | Allen. The name stays **Forge** (not Furnace) and "Forge is full" stays. Replaces the dot texture, shimmer, rarity glow and "Select Ingot" titles | [[Sessions/2026-10-18]] |
| 2026-10-18 | **Forge Take out uses the same E prompt** ("Use forge" becomes "Take out" when your ingot is ready) instead of a second prompt | Two E prompts at one station would fight over the key | [[Sessions/2026-10-18]] |
| 2026-10-18 | **Gotham font** for the game UI (pickers, toasts, notices, forge timer, seat panel) | Allen. Replaces Fredoka / Nunito in Studio; Figma keeps them since Gotham isn't in Figma | [[Sessions/2026-10-18]] |
| 2026-10-18 | **Anvil: choose the hot ingot first, then the shape** from a picker of black blade silhouettes (priced for that metal) | Allen; replaces shape-first from earlier the same day. Dropping the < > buttons is Claude's default | [[Sessions/2026-10-18]] |
| 2026-10-18 | **Hot spots (concept 4)** prototyped as the main candidate and made the anvil's default mode: timed strikes on glowing parts of the edges, every run makes a sword, timing sets the grade | Allen asked for it after the anvil friction review; the other modes stay switchable (M) for the playtest | [[Sessions/2026-10-18]] |
| 2026-10-18 | **Silhouette becomes hand-forging**: stronger hammer blows, a Grind tool (drag an edge, the fixing step), and heat that drops per blow with Reheat; it's the default anvil mode again | Allen: Hot spots left no room to fix mistakes and didn't feel hand-made; real smithing is hammer, grind, reheat. Supersedes "metal does not cool" for this mode | [[Sessions/2026-10-18]] |
| 2026-10-18 | Reheat happens **at the anvil** (3 s) for now | Prototype shortcut; carrying the blade back to the forge is still open | [[Sessions/2026-10-18]] |
| 2026-10-18 | **Quality is a percentage, no grade names** (no Crude / Fine / Superior / Masterwork). Price multiplier grows smoothly: ×1.0 at 80% → ×2.0 at 100%; the Knight likes 90%+ | Allen: "I don't need the masterwork label. It should just be percentages." Keeping the price link and the smooth curve are Claude's defaults | [[Sessions/2026-10-18]] |
| 2026-10-18 | **Strike point:** in tapping modes the hammer always lands on the anvil face's centre and the blade slides so the tapped part is under it | Allen's idea, so a tap reads as hammering that part of the blade | [[Sessions/2026-10-18]] |
| 2026-10-18 | **Guided Hot spots (option A):** the ring stays fixed on the strike point and the game moves the blade under it: near edge hilt→tip, flip, far edge tip→hilt; tip parts turned 90°. Hot spots is the default anvil mode while Allen tries it | Allen: points stay still, the blade moves, with turns and flips for interest. Chosen over the player dragging the blade (option B). Silhouette keeps free aim | [[Sessions/2026-10-18]] |
| 2026-10-18 | **The fixed-point idea belongs to Points (concept 3)**: Hot spots merged into Points and removed. Points keeps 12 points, 10 s cooling and 6+ hits, now one at a time on the fixed strike point with the blade slid / turned / flipped under it | Allen: "I was thinking of this for the method 3"; chose the merge over varied spots or keeping both | [[Sessions/2026-10-18]] |
| 2026-10-18 | Points: **each hit dents its own part** of the blade (misses leave that part wide); a strike while the blade is still moving is a miss | Follows from "hitting the point = hammering that part"; supersedes the 2026-10-17 "partly formed" even morph for Points. Claude's defaults | [[Sessions/2026-10-18]] |
| 2026-10-18 | **Katana redrawn from real katana proportions** (smooth curve, proper pointed tip, ~1.7× real width so it's playable), replacing the Creator Store trace | Allen: the silhouettes looked off; the trace had a zig-zag spine and a blunt tip. The curve is ~1.9% of the length (real ~2.6%): each edge must stay on its side of the blade's axis, so the axis runs hilt-to-point and held swords turn the blade ~7.6° at the guard | [[Sessions/2026-10-18]] |
| 2026-10-18 | **Accent lines redone**: the ridge sits across the blade's own width on a smoothed path, lines are a darker shade of the metal; the Katana gets a single shinogi ridge (~1/3 from the spine) and a yokote line | Allen: the Katana's line looked like a sharpie: it hugged one edge (measured from the off-centre axis), copied hammer dents and was a fixed dark grey | [[Sessions/2026-10-18]] |
| 2026-10-18 | **Historical shop asset: medieval log cabin** | Superseded by the open-front Japanese workshop on 2026-10-19; retain model as an earlier asset | [[Sessions/2026-10-18]] |

## Historical questions

The earlier open questions about reheat, timed Points, Trace, and dagger starting prices are superseded by the 2026-10-19 direction. Historical prototype behavior remains in the session notes and archived GDD. Storage capacity and new progression prices still need design/tuning.

## 2026-10-19 — Japanese village and simplified crafting

This is chronological history, not an implementation tracker. Later decisions supersede earlier choices on the same topic. See [[Design/Game Design Document]] for current rules and [[Current Status]] for build status.

| Date | Decision | Details | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-10-19 | **Setting and tone** | Japanese mountain swordsmith village inspired by Swordsmith Village; cozy daily life with spectacular individual visitors. Village-wide events deferred. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Workshop** | Open-front Japanese workshop, visible forging and displays facing the path; expand one increasingly elaborate workshop. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Starting blueprints — superseded/clarified** | Tantō → wakizashi → katana, taught through local commissions that provide the pattern when each request begins. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Early customers** | Mostly locals plus an early special outsider hinting at the wider world. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Commissions — superseded/clarified** | In-person requests and letters from returning customers replace smartphone texts. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Materials** | Keep the random Ore Roller, redesigned for the village, and the full fantasy material variety available from the start. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Wider traditions** | Visitors introduce new sword traditions through a first commission with its pattern; buy additional blueprints within that tradition. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Customer arrivals** | Displayed stock influences regular visitors; reputation unlocks surprise special visitors. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Special requests** | Untimed commissions; special visitors return when the requested sword is ready. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Customer tastes** | Regular customers have flexible tastes; special commissions specify combinations. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Haggling** | Retain negotiation and the risk that a customer leaves when pushed for a higher offer. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Fittings** | Match each sword tradition; unlock through crafting milestones, without a later coin-purchase requirement. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Scope** | Craft, collect, display, and sell; no combat or testing area for now. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Automation** | Keep both cashier and apprentice smith as later upgrades. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Decoration** | Functional displays and facilities improve business; purely decorative items are cosmetic. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Relationships** | Repeat sales build familiarity; milestones unlock personal commission chains and rewards. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Character roster** | Original village regulars plus recognizable fictional and historical special visitors. Leave their coexistence unexplained. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Quality** | Keep visible percentages and a price link, without named quality tiers. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Forging** | Hit every circle; each hit improves quality. No time limit, timing windows, misses, or cooling countdown. Supersedes earlier timed-strike/retry discussion. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Grinding** | All circles gives 90%; optional freehand grinding improves toward 100%. Remove only excess metal, stop at the target, never reduce quality. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Hilt assembly** | Three untimed taps, without timing bonuses or penalties. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Always open pickers** | Every item-selection interaction opens its picker, even with one valid item. No automatic current-workpiece selection or picker bypass. Supersedes the review recommendation. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **10-second base heating** | Forge heating takes 10 seconds, replacing the 15-second prototype duration. Implementation pending. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **First customer arrives after listing** | No customers visit the player’s shop during the first sword craft. Putting the sword on sale triggers the tutorial customer arrival. Replaces the proposed pre-craft/waiting customer; later sales-interruption policy remains open. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Starting tantō blueprint and guidance** | Players start with the tantō blueprint and simple on-screen instructions. Later local commissions introduce wakizashi and katana. Supersedes giving the first tantō pattern through a customer commission. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **First sale guaranteed fair; haggle on second — superseded/clarified** | First tutorial customer gives a guaranteed fair offer. Introduce haggling on the second sale, keeping the first reward straightforward. Exact fair-price calculation remains untuned. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Wakizashi commission after first sale** | The next goal after selling the first tantō is a local wakizashi teaching commission, providing its blueprint at commission start. No required upgrade purchase or additional tantō sale first. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Wakizashi commission specifies a rollable material — superseded/clarified** | Require a specific material available from the player’s current Ore Roller, rather than accepting any material. Exact material undecided; no guaranteed drop or odds change approved. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **First commission: Iron Wakizashi** | Iron chosen for the wakizashi teaching commission after the first tantō sale. Iron is in the starter roller’s Common pool. No guaranteed drop or altered odds approved. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Deliver commissions through a picker** | Select the completed sword through the commission and receive the agreed reward, without listing or haggling. Applies to the first Iron Wakizashi. Haggling tutorial therefore belongs to the second ordinary shop sale, not commission delivery. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Iron Wakizashi: minimum 90% quality** | Completing every forging circle meets the first commission’s quality requirement. Grinding remains optional; higher-quality swords also qualify. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **First upgrade after Iron Wakizashi — superseded/clarified** | Completing the Iron Wakizashi commission leads into the first workshop upgrade, introducing spending earnings to grow the business. Upgrade type and price remain undecided. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **First upgrade: second Ore Roller — superseded/clarified** | Each roller yields one material per roll; two rollers provide more chances to obtain a desirable material. This adds a roller rather than changing per-roll rarity odds. Price and activation/cooldown behavior remain undecided. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Automatic rollers with one waiting output — superseded/clarified** | Each roller automatically produces one material, waits until it is collected, then starts its next roll. Replaces manual hold-to-roll activation. Roll duration remains undecided. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Lever-operated ore roulette; under five seconds — superseded/clarified** | Player activates the lever; the stand rapidly cycles ore appearances and stops on the rolled material. Suggested visual method: change the displayed material. Result waits for collection; no automatic restart. Supersedes the earlier automatic-roller decision. Exact reveal duration remains untuned. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **One lever, multiple material stands** | Buying another roller adds a stand, not a lever. The shared lever rolls all empty stands together; occupied stands retain their ores and cannot roll until collected. More stands give more results per activation. Supersedes separate-lever wording. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Grounded starter blades, extravagant later designs** | Tantō, wakizashi, and katana establish the craft with distinct silhouettes and slightly exaggerated readable proportions. Later special blueprints introduce unusual fantasy shapes. Exact profiles and shaping choreography remain open. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Staged blade shaping** | Early hammer hits lengthen the blank, middle hits establish taper and curve, and final hits form the point and refine the outline. Each impact visibly changes the area beneath the hammer. Exact intermediate profiles, hit counts, and pacing remain open. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Blade repositions without tongs** | The blade automatically slides, turns, and flips beneath the hammer between strikes, with no visible tongs or manual positioning. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Smooth localized impact deformation** | Each hammer impact quickly deforms the struck area and blends into neighboring metal. Flatten/stretch according to the stage; avoid shape snapping, wobbling, and slow melting. Timing and falloff remain untuned. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **90% blade: clean, subtly rough** | Correct recognizable silhouette, subtle excess metal along the edge, and a dull finish. Grinding refines it; skipping grinding should still yield an attractive sword after assembly. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **One universal starting blank** | All sword blueprints start from the same blank shape and size, then stretch to the chosen blade’s length during forging. Material appearance still follows the rolled material. Replaces proposed blueprint-sized blanks. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Equal initial hit count; modest tier effort later** | Start with the same hit count across sword blueprints. Higher-tier swords should eventually take a bit more effort. Exact scaling and form of extra effort remain undecided; preserve untimed forging. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Angled overhead forging camera** | Slightly angled overhead view preserves silhouette readability, blade thickness, hammer movement, and anvil depth. Keep the full blade visible as it lengthens and repositions. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Starter blades use real lengths and curvature** | Tantō 25.8 cm with a slight inward curve (sori −0.1 cm), wakizashi 50 cm (sori 1.2 cm), katana 70 cm (sori 1.8 cm), measured the catalogue way. Representative values from research; sori varies by period. Widths, thickness, kissaki and tangs remain exploratory. Sources in [[Development/Blade Profile Authoring|Blade Profile Authoring]]. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Smithy art direction: A/B mix** | Allen chose a mix of the two directions in [[Archive/Art/Smithy Redesign|Smithy Redesign]]: Direction A's materials and colours (white plaster, dark charred-cedar timber, grey tile roof, stone, iron, small vermilion and indigo accents) with Direction B's chunkier proportions (thicker posts and trim, bolder silhouettes) and extra lanterns (one on every front post). | [[Archive/Art/Smithy Redesign|Smithy Redesign]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Station shapes for the rebuild** | The proposal's defaults, which Allen didn't change: hornless kanatoko anvil (`Face` and `BladeMount` unchanged), upright-sword pedestals as sword holders, an "ore shrine" Ore Roller with stone stands and one shared lever, warm late-afternoon lighting, old prototype blocks moved to `ServerStorage.Archive`, cabin files moved to `Models/Archive/`. | [[Archive/Art/Smithy Redesign|Smithy Redesign]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Workshop built; choices made while building** | Claude's defaults, made during the build: holders at x 9.8 / 13 / 16.2 (3.2 apart, not 5, so the middle one stays at ShopService's inspect point), the Ore Roller lever post moved to x −12.2 and the stands to x ≈ −15, SpawnLocation moved to (1.5, 0.15, 9) as a low stone slab, Baseplate turned to grass, lighting set to ClockTime 16.4 with warm ambient (was 14.5 with grey 70/70/70). The anvil `Face` was darkened to 30/31/36 because the cooled steel blade was hard to see on it under the warm light. The noren were shortened to 1.4 studs so they don't block the player camera. | [[Archive/Art/Smithy Redesign|Smithy Redesign]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Starter blueprints replace the prototype set** | The game's blueprints are now the tantō, wakizashi and katana from the blade data, replacing Dagger, Shortsword and the old Katana. Placeholder prices 20 / 45 / 110 coins and 1–3 stars until the economy pass. The Adventurer now likes wakizashi (was Shortsword); the Knight likes 95%+ blades (was 90%, which every forged blade now reaches). | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **First-person hammering arm deferred** | Tried a first-person view with the character's arm swinging the hammer; Allen chose to leave the arm for later as unimportant for now. The anvil keeps the angled overhead camera with the hammer in view (pivoting from behind the anvil so it never covers the blade). | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Larger plot with storefront and crafting area** | Each player needs a spacious property with distinct retail and crafting spaces. L-shaped connected wings are a visual proposal; exact layout and dimensions remain open. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Visible early/mid/late plot growth** | Sparse shop/basic stations early; more swords and stations mid game; expanded shop, workers, and advanced upgrades late game. Concept capacities remain illustrative. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Player plot built to the L-shaped concept; choices made while building** | Allen asked to implement the plot concept. Claude's defaults: the existing workshop stays as the covered crafting wing; a new storefront wing (25 × 38 studs, gable facing the road) joins it on the left; the three sword pedestals and the 刀鍛冶 sign moved into the storefront; plot is 126 × 58 studs (x −50…76, z −28…30) with a paved courtyard, an expansion lawn on the right, a fenced boundary and one entrance in line with the storefront door; the village road runs along the front at z 34…46. **Deviations from the concept:** the anvil and ore stands stay under the crafting roof rather than out in the courtyard (forging camera already tuned there); bamboo and greenery are simple parts. Customers now spawn and inspect at marker parts (`Village.CustomerSpawn`, `DisplayRack.InspectPoint`) instead of hard-coded positions. Built from Studio parts; Blender was not needed, and there is no MCP path to import a mesh into Studio. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Plot v2 from the Player Plot Review; choices made while building** | Allen asked to work on [[Archive/Art/Player Plot Review|Player Plot Review]]. Claude's defaults: a new 50 × 28 crafting pavilion replaces the shallow workshop (archived); forge, ore shrine and hilt bench along its back wall with the anvil on open floor in front of the forge; ore shrine and anvil stay under the roof; plot deepened to 126 × 78 by moving the back fence (frontage and road unchanged); holders become three tall pedestals in two columns with an aisle to the counter, each with its own customer stand point; **no Z-fighting** as a standing build rule (fixed ground-layer heights, faces 0.04 proud where parts meet), from Allen's note in `Random thoughts.md`. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Stations restyled low-poly and cartoony in Blender (proposal)** | Allen asked for low-poly stations with bright cartoony colours. Claude's defaults: same five stations and shapes as the Studio rebuild, flat-shaded chamfered primitives, one bright palette (teal roofs, vermilion, cream plaster, orange wood, gold trim, lavender stone); the anvil face stays dark navy so the hot blade still reads. Built in Blender in `Models/Stations/`, not yet imported; the Studio parts that scripts read stay as they are. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Blender stations v2 follow the station review and concept sheet** | Allen asked to build from [[Archive/Art/Blender Station Review|Blender Station Review]] and `blender-station-concepts-v1.png`. The ore shrine roof and frame are gone: each ore stand is its own repeatable `OreStand` model and the one shared lever is a separate `OreLever` model. The display pedestal becomes an open `SwordHolder` (tip socket, collar under the guard). The anvil is a thick iron block on timber with no accessories; the forge loses its ornaments. Bright cartoony palette kept, with fewer accent colours (no teal roofs, gold only on one tsuba). | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Blender stations v3: choices made while building (proposals)** | From Allen's v2 feedback. Claude's defaults: anvil face lowered to 2.3 above the floor for posture (Studio today 3.0; needs the Anvil station moved down 0.7 and a playtest); sword hangs by its guard on a fixed collar because the game centres the grip on the Slot, so the collar fits all three blades while the tip rest only meets the katana (holder slot would rise to about 4.43 above the floor); ore-stand texture test uses tiled painted 512 px PNGs, one mesh per texture, `Ore` kept flat for recolouring. Anvil height, holder slot height and the texture style await Allen. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Universal sword holder carries the guard; no tip socket** | Allen: shorter blades hung above an unused socket. The holder now carries the guard on padded crossbar halves with a slot for the blade, so every blade length fits. Still tall because of the katana; sized holders or wall holders for short blades remain open. Also from the same review: texture variation per part (own UV offset and scale) instead of more density; iron wear stays restrained. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Stations move to mid-high poly** | Allen asked for mid-high poly. Claude's defaults: same designs, rounded 3-segment bevels with smooth shading, 28+ sided cylinders, faceted ore kept flat-shaded; low-poly stays available through one `DETAIL` switch and its exports are kept. Replaces the earlier low-poly direction for the Blender stations. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Detailed katana hilt modeled (proposal)** | Allen asked for a detailed hilt from the reference katanas. Claude's defaults: Japanese koshirae (wrapped tsuka, menuki, fuchi, kashira, rimmed tsuba, habaki) in the station palette, grip 1.2 studs (today's in-game grip is 0.75), indigo wrap over cream with gold fittings. Not wired into `buildSword`; recipe variants and the grip length are open. See [[Art/Katana Hilt]]. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Blender models pass the Roblox QA check and use the Roblox export preset** | Allen agreed to adopt the installed `blender-asset-qa` and `blender-export-pipelines` skills. Every model is checked with `qa_check.py --profile roblox` before export and exported with the `roblox` FBX preset instead of the hand-rolled settings. Accepted exceptions: soot decals sit 0.02 off the surface; hidden back-to-back faces and UV overlap on untextured parts. 1 Blender unit = 1 stud is unverified until the scale test is imported in Studio. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Katana hilt v2: approved flat-band wrap, grip fitted to whole crossings** | Allen approved the wrap test. Claude's defaults while building: four crossings at the approved 0.24 pitch so the wrap starts and ends on a crossing (grip 1.08 instead of 1.2), slimmer fittings shaped to the flat grip, menuki placed in windows. Grip length versus the game's 0.75 `GRIP_LENGTH` is still open. See [[Art/Katana Hilt]]. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Every sword uses the detailed katana hilt; recipes become colour schemes** | Allen asked to bring the hilt into the game. Claude's defaults: one hilt mesh for all six recipes, coloured per recipe (cord = grip, fittings = guard, end cap = pommel); the old western guard widths and pommel sizes no longer drive the look; grip 1.082 and blade offset 0.613 replace 0.75 / 0.85. Meshes reach Studio through the Open Cloud Assets API rather than manual import. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Flame etch prototype: etch as a blade skin plus a matching guard (proposal)** | From the main session's brief. Claude's defaults: an original flame motif (not the Rengoku design); the etch is a procedural two-colour skin built from the blade's own station params rather than a texture, so it fits every blueprint and grind; a sword carries `etch = { pattern, body, flame, guard? }`; a matching alternate guard (`SwordParts.<name>Guard`) replaces the tsuba and rim and takes the etch colours. Flames reach at most ~60% of the blade width. Etching Table, purchases and minigame not started. See [[Art/Flame Etch]]. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-20 | **Etching Table: free and instant for now; ore stands spin and hold results** | Allen chose "apply instantly" (trace minigame later) and "both free for now" (no table or pattern prices). Claude's defaults: Flame adds +15% value (GDD §6.3 Etching/Rune); the table sits on the pavilion's back wall between the forge and the Hilt Bench; re-etching just replaces the pattern; the etched Tool returns to the backpack. Ore stands: 2.6 s flicker that slows to a stop, result held with a name/rarity label until its roller collects it with E (others are told it isn't theirs), empty stands hide their ore, a full set of stands refuses to roll. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-20 | **Flame etch v3 proposed; habaki made smaller** | Allen asked for connected, curved flame tongues from a narrow edge band, with varied size and spacing, a dark body and a metallic bevel, shown first as a flat preview. Claude's reading: a bare steel strip along the cutting edge (16%), a narrow band, then S-curved tongues leaning to the hilt. The alternative is flames only above the ridge. Waiting for Allen's approval before the game changes. Habaki resized to 0.17 × 0.12 × 0.052 to sit just over the blade root. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-20 | **Flame etch v4: match Allen's reference band** | Allen supplied a screenshot as the visual target, replacing the v3 tongues: a red band along the cutting edge with a shallow, rounded, uneven boundary, a mostly dark metallic blade, thin bright edges and bevel shading. Values measured from the screenshot: band at 28% of the width, humps every 0.75–1.05 widths, amplitude 10–17%, an occasional small point. Shown side by side at the reference's orientation; waiting for approval before the game changes. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-20 | **Flame etch v4 approved and in the game, with even peaks** | Allen asked why some peaks were smaller (random heights copied from the reference, plus small points) and chose even peaks: every hump is 0.14 of the width and only the spacing varies. Approved with "Even peaks look right. Implement v4." Kept the vertex-coloured skin instead of the planned EditableImage, since a single crisp boundary plus per-vertex shading covers it. Etch colours are now dark navy 6,12,20 and red 165,14,10. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-20 | **One guard shape per hilt recipe; real hilts on the Hilt Bench cards** | Allen asked for more hilts with different guards and a simple handle, and for the hilt station to show the handle and guard. Rim = guard colour, inlay = pommel colour. `SwordParts` moved to ReplicatedStorage so clients can draw the real parts. Adding new recipes with their own bonuses and unlocks is still open. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-20 | **Blender stations replace the part-built ones; swords hang blade-down; icons match the item** | Allen asked to import the Blender stations and replace the existing ones, and for icons to match their description ("if it says katana, it should show the katana blade"). Claude's defaults on the open questions: anvil height per the v3 proposal (station 0.7 lower); the universal v4 holder everywhere, so displayed swords hang blade-down with the guard on the crossbar (sized or wall holders for short blades are still open); blade and sword cards draw the real blueprint blade on a shared square-root scale, 1.5× wider for readability. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-20 | **Polishing Station is the final step; the anvil only forges** | Allen: polishing happens on the completely built sword as a final polish, not at the anvil. His answers: polish is the final step (after hilt and etching), remove grinding from the anvil, keep the current freehand mechanic, available from the start. Claude's defaults: free; fully polished swords can't be picked again; etching a polished sword is still allowed; the server refuses anvil blades above 90%. Also from Allen's references: a reroll lever that swings while E is held, and a much larger ore display. | [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-20 | **Stations in step order; ore shown as an ingot; swords share meshes** | Allen: "make the ore generated look like an ingot. Also order the stations in the order that makes sense". Laid out in one row in that order; Allen then asked for it "left to right" and no signs, so it now runs 1 Ore Roller → 6 Polishing Station left to right as seen from the courtyard, starting beside the shop. To fit the EditableMesh budget, displayed swords draw polish at 0/50/100% and etch meshes are colour-neutral. | [[Development/Code Map]] |
| 2026-10-20 | **A pull always takes 4 seconds** | Allen: the roll should take the same time however many ores. Claude's defaults: 1.5 s in + 2.5 s out; fixed trip lengths (far carts roll faster); with one cart the spare time is a pause in the dark rather than a slow crawl. | [[Development/Code Map]] |
| 2026-10-20 | **Six ore carts on a turning track, in the game** | Allen: max 6 carts/rolls, three straight and three after a right turn; then swap it in and roll the carts in and out one by one. | 2026-10-20 | **Mine entrance instead of a shaft (design)** | Allen: carts should come out of the ground, one by one, on the lever; reference a Clash of Clans gold mine. Allen approved the sketch and asked to swap it in. Claude's defaults: laid per face in studs and baked into 1024² colour + OpenGL normal maps; door origin on its hinge line; a headless build script. | [[Archive/Art/Blender Station Review]] |
| 2026-10-20 | **Display table carries a sword stand** | Allen's reference photo: a katana stand on a counter. Then: "remove the hood, it should just be a resuable sword holder, that players can purchase to place more of on the wall". No hood; one 5.2 × 1.7 unit that tiles along a wall. Claude's defaults: same shop footprint; the crafting area moves 8 studs east as an open-air pavilion (roof on posts, low lattice screen at the back) on a paved stone courtyard; the shop gets a side door onto it. Hitting every circle gives 100% (×2.0); a full polish adds +10% as a finish bonus and names the sword "(Polished)". The anvil view closes after the showcase. | [[Design/Game Design Document]] |

| 2026-10-20 | **One-piece anvil; unlimited re-rolls; wider shops; roof fades inside** | Allen asked for an anvil that looks like one piece (reference image), unlimited ore re-rolls, wider Starter and Upgraded Stores, and a roof that disappears while you're inside. Claude's defaults: Upgraded 31 × 38, Starter 24 × 14; only the roof fades, walls stay. | [[Archive/Art/Building Redesign]] |

Claude's defaults: Ingot Rack holds 12, Sword Rack holds 6, carrying is capped at 6 each, store and take through the item picker. | [[Development/Code Map]] |
| 2026-10-20 | **Polish before etch; simpler forge and ore displays** | From Allen's quick thoughts: "etching should be the last step since it will be an unlocked station"; the forge shows one tray and only a timer, then "Ready!"; the anvil's Leave [Q] button says Cancel; the ore display is simpler and 50% larger, with the ore floating while it rolls. Claude's defaults: Polishing Station and Etching Table swapped places; a plain two-tone round stand (dark wood base and cap, plaster column), no sparkles; Q still cancels. | [[Random thoughts]] |

It replaces the old rack mesh on the template and WallRack1–4. ## Growth decisions — 2026-10-19

The following growth decisions were organized from Allen's inbox and direct answers on 2026-10-19. Existing rows above retain their originally recorded dates, including entries labeled 2026-10-20; this pass does not redate historical session records.

| Date | Decision | Why / notes | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-10-19 | **Employee hiring, levels and separate production roles** | Add extra anvils, an un-hilted blade rack and employee management/upgrade UI. Hires differ in speed/quality and progress through completed work. Heater, smith and hilter are separate roles; smiths leave bare blades on the rack rather than automatically attaching hilts. Worker polishing has its own ceiling; the dedicated polishing hire is awaiting clarification. Exact prices, XP, caps and assignment rules remain open. | [[Design/Workshop Growth]]; [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Starter smiths train from below 90%; players can complete both stages** | Allen chose lower employee starting quality progressing toward 90%, and player recovery: finish sub-90% un-hilted blades at the anvil to 90%, then polish assembled swords beyond worker polish limits up to 100% at the Polishing Station. The old fixed 85% apprentice example is superseded. Player forging remains 90%; sub-90% data/value/refinement rules need design before implementation. | [[Design/Workshop Growth#Employees and workstations]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Separate optional courier delivery board** | Allen selected a separate delivery board and village courier/cart. Order → make swords → pack → dispatch → return → collect payout; pin orders; speed/capacity are upgrade directions. Existing in-person/letter commission picker delivery, including the first Iron Wakizashi, stays intact. Pottery's van / 3:30 / 3000 gold / 6→9 are references only. | [[Design/Workshop Growth#Optional remote delivery orders]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Weather gives traffic bonuses only** | Allen chose sunny-weather bonuses with other weather at normal baseline; bad weather does not reduce arrivals below baseline. Preserve the first-sale customer gate. Exact modifiers, schedule and stacking remain open. | [[Design/Workshop Growth#Weather and customer traffic]] |
| 2026-10-19 | **Roof transparency/cutaway while indoors** | Allen requested roof transparency when under the roof. Reference screenshots show opaque exterior and a cutaway interior. Local smooth fade/hide with exterior restoration is the recommended implementation, not a new numeric opacity requirement. | [[Design/Workshop Growth#Roof visibility while indoors]] |

## Employee roster decision — 2026-10-19

| Date | Decision | Why / notes | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-10-19 | **Five employee professions: furnace operator, smith, finisher, stocker, cashier** | Allen approved the proposed stocker after combining hilting and optional polishing into the finisher. This supersedes the earlier separate-hilter / undecided-polisher split. Smiths deposit bare blades on one rack; finishers assemble, pause for any player etch, optionally polish and release ready swords to a separate finished rack. Stockers visibly fill empty holders from eligible ready-for-sale stock, preserving commission/delivery/further-finishing reservations and waiting when full or empty. Stocking-speed progression initially; priority modes remain later options. Multiple workers can share a profession; exact caps and values stay open. Courier remains part of deliveries; no ore attendant or automated etcher selected. | [[Design/Workshop Growth#Employees and workstations]]; [[Sessions/2026-10-19]] |

## How to read superseded entries

The five-profession decision above resolves the initial hilter/polisher question. Earlier rows and the first organization session retain the wording that was true when recorded. The latest roster, racks and retail handoff are authoritative in GDD §10.4 and [[Design/Workshop Growth]].

Rows marked **superseded/clarified** remain as history. Current clarifications: the loot box uses one shared lever and requires all waiting ore to be collected before rerolling; the starting tantō pattern is available immediately; the first commission is Iron Wakizashi at 90% minimum; direct commission delivery is separate from ordinary shop sales and optional courier orders; the first tutorial upgrade adds a second ore slot; completing player forging gives 100% quality and optional polishing adds a separate +10% finish bonus. Employee limits and recovery need reconciliation in [[Design/Workshop Growth]]. Read the GDD for complete current behavior. Proposed changes in [[Archive/Design/Core Loop Review]] are not decisions until approved.

## World and workshop decisions — 2026-10-21 to 2026-10-22

| Date | Decision | Why / notes | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-10-21 | **The map is a Japanese town street** | Allen's references (Kawagoe, Takayama, Tsumago, Gifu, Kanazawa): one straight street of two-storey machiya, lattice fronts, pent roofs, noren and lanterns, mountains at the ends. Built as `Workspace.Village` with six 140 × 110 plot lots opening onto it, a village gate at the west end and a shrine at the east end; layout, lot size and placement are a first pass for Allen's review. | [[Sessions/2026-10-21]], [[Archive/Art/Japanese Street]] |
| 2026-10-21 | **Shops are street townhouses** | Allen: the shops should blend with the street's houses and match their style. Both shops are part-built machiya on the street's façade line in lot N2 (Starter: one storey; Upgraded Store: black-plaster kura with a pent-roof signboard). This replaces the bright feudal shells (vermilion posts, sky-blue sori roofs) of 2026-10-20. | [[Sessions/2026-10-21]], [[Archive/Art/Japanese Street#Shops and workshop (2026-10-21)]] |
| 2026-10-21 | **Smelting and smithing move indoors** | Allen: "we can have the smelting and sword creation process inside a building as well". A 74 × 71 workshop hall now encloses the stations behind a courtyard, so each plot is a shop on the street plus a workshop. This supersedes the 2026-10-20 "stations outdoors on a courtyard" layout and the GDD's open-front single workshop. | [[Sessions/2026-10-21]] |
| 2026-10-21 | **Mountains removed for now** | Allen: "remove the mountains, we will work on it later". The cliff ring is parked; the invisible boundary walls stay. | [[Sessions/2026-10-21]] |
| 2026-10-21 | **Shop + back-room workshop in one building** | Allen's layout (`References/Concept Art/shop design`): shop on the street, workshop door, stations clockwise (ore intake → furnace → anvil → hilt bench → optional polish → optional etch → finished rack by the door), ingot storage on the left wall. Claude's defaults, which Allen accepted: 48 wide (shop room 28 deep, workshop 50); the upgrade changes only the front; Furnace 2 beside the furnace; extra ore slots in the alcove; the roof fades over whichever room you're in; the separate hall, courtyard and mine are parked. Supersedes the separate workshop hall of earlier the same day. | [[Sessions/2026-10-21]], [[Archive/Art/Japanese Street#Shop and back-room workshop (2026-10-21)]] |
| 2026-10-21 | **Placeholder supply alcove for ore** | Allen is still choosing how ore arrives (Claude suggested a tatara furnace, an ore peddler, an iron-sand sluice, sealed crucibles or a shrine draw). Until then the lever rolls up to six ore trays on a stage in the workshop corner, in place, with the same timing; the carts' upgrade ids stay, named "Ore Slot". | [[Sessions/2026-10-21]] |
| 2026-10-21 | **Players start with one furnace** | Allen: "remove Furnace2 from Smithy.Forge so players start with one furnace and Forge2 stays the upgrade". `Forge` had been tagged as part of the Furnace 2 upgrade by mistake. | [[Sessions/2026-10-21]] |
| 2026-10-21 | **Lots fit the building; closed shopfronts** | Allen: "shrink lot N2 to the building, bring the parked townhouses back, resize all six lots to the new footprint, and give unclaimed lots a closed shopfront so the street reads as one continuous row." Lots are 48.5 × 96; the street was rebuilt with townhouses between them; unclaimed lots get a fully shuttered townhouse. | [[Sessions/2026-10-21]], [[Archive/Art/Japanese Street#Continuous row (2026-10-21)]] |
| 2026-10-21 | **Six player plots** | Allen approved the plan with Claude's defaults: one template (the plot in lot N2) copied onto a lot when a player joins, lots handed out N2, N1, N3, S2, S1, S3; visitors can walk into other plots but not use anything; a plot is deleted (upgrades, displays, furnace and ore contents) when its owner leaves, until saving exists; 6 players per server. | [[Design/Six Plots]], [[Sessions/2026-10-21]] |
| 2026-10-21 | **The Upgraded Store opens up the storeroom — superseded** | Allen picked option 1 of three (storeroom, upstairs showroom, the house next door). The Starter has a 12-deep storeroom between shop and workshop; the upgrade removes its wall, so the shop room grows from 28 to 40 deep. The workshop moved 12 back; the Upgraded-only displays stand in the new space. | [[Archive/Art/Japanese Street#Shop grows on the upgrade (2026-10-21)]], [[Sessions/2026-10-21]] |
| 2026-10-21 | **The upgrade pushes the workshop back** | Allen: "ideally, the shop expands upon upgrade the blacksmith area just gets pushed further back instead of our current version". No storeroom: the Starter's shop room meets the workshop; buying the Upgraded Store moves the workshop, its stations, pads, the player spawn (and anyone inside) 12 studs back, and the shop room becomes 40 deep. Supersedes the storeroom row above. | [[Archive/Art/Japanese Street#Shop grows on the upgrade (2026-10-21)]], [[Sessions/2026-10-21]] |
| 2026-10-21 | **Both roofs fade together** | Allen: "while in their building, can you make both roofs disppear so players can see their shop and their blacksmith stations". Standing in the shop or the workshop now fades the roofs of every building on that plot, so both stay open while you walk between them; roofs come back when you step outside. | [[Sessions/2026-10-21]] |
| 2026-10-21 | **The street is wider** | Allen: "Can we also expand the street a bit, it seems very closed and tight right now". Fronts 44 apart instead of 32: only the road grew (28 wide), so walkways, gutters and each plot's front are unchanged; the north row stays, the south row moves 12 south. | [[Archive/Art/Japanese Street#Wider street, taller buildings (2026-10-21)]], [[Sessions/2026-10-21]] |
| 2026-10-21 | **Ground storeys 2.5 taller** | Allen: "also make the buildings a bit taller, right now the entrance is barely taller than the player". Every house, the shop and the workshop: doorways reach 9.7 (were 7.2), shop storey 13.5, workshop eave 15.5. Upper storeys and roofs unchanged. | [[Archive/Art/Japanese Street#Wider street, taller buildings (2026-10-21)]], [[Sessions/2026-10-21]] |
| 2026-10-21 | **Shop sign shows the owner's name** | Allen: "the sign outside of the plot should have players name on it not that japanese text". The signboard over each player's shop (Starter and Upgraded) shows their display name. | [[Archive/Project History/Code Map before cleanup — 2026-10-22#Six player plots (2026-10-21)]], [[Sessions/2026-10-21]] |
| 2026-10-21 | **Custom hotbar** | Allen: "can you implement a custom toolbar that replaces roblox's default one. I'm thinking something like this. except the background is a translucent black, with 0 corner radius. a darker black border around it. if you have the same ingots, they should stack and say 2x, or 3x, or whatever it is." Replaces Roblox's Backpack bar: translucent black square slots with a darker border, ingots stack by metal and heat with a count, swords one per slot. No inventory button or gamepad cycling yet. | [[Art/UI Design#Hotbar (2026-10-21)]], [[Sessions/2026-10-21]] |
| 2026-10-21 | **Hotbar slots in rarity colours** | Allen, after live comparisons: "Use rarity colours with a black 1px item outline and ink-black slot edges and text strokes, corner halftone, L size with hilt, 10px corners, bright lighting." Replaces the translucent black square slots of the first hotbar. | [[Art/UI Design#Hotbar look, chosen (2026-10-21)]], [[Sessions/2026-10-21]] |
| 2026-10-21 | **Hotbar gets a bag** | Allen: "can you implement a backpack so when players have over 9 in their toolbar, they can drag things into the bag or they overfill into the bag. like in the screenshot i had sent before." The bar holds 9 stacks plus the bag button (his backpack icon, key ~); overflow goes to the bag, a grid above the bar; drag to swap, stash or bring back. Order is kept per client. | [[Art/UI Design#Backpack (2026-10-21)]], [[Sessions/2026-10-21]] |
| 2026-10-21 | **Etching is shown, not traced (v1)** | Allen asked to implement the animated-etching handoff: the sword lies on the player's own table and a stylus draws the pattern with the marks appearing behind it; automatic viewing, no minigame. Eclipse is the first stroked pattern; Flame keeps its look and wipes in along the blade; the etch stays free, instant and server-applied, the show is cosmetic and local. Stroked marks bake the material colour (a texture ignores the part's Color). | [[Art/Animated Blade Etching#Built (2026-10-21)]], [[Sessions/2026-10-21]] |
| 2026-10-21 | **Finished swords use baked meshes** | Allen asked what the EditableMesh limit means, then: "yes that'd be ideal and make there's an established way so that we can add more swords later", and after the spike: "Go ahead and build the bake tool, bake the swords and switch SwordMeshCache to baked-first." Every blueprint × polish level × blade, etch overlay and Flame skin is an uploaded mesh. Live EditableMeshes remain only at the anvil and Polishing Station, and for shapes not baked yet. Adding a sword: blueprint, bake, spec, save. See [[Development/Sword Mesh Pipeline]]. |
| 2026-10-21 | **One etch per sword** | Allen: "i noticed that once i've etched something, im still able to etch it?" An etched sword can't be etched again: `EtchService` refuses it, and the Etching Table's sword picker shows it locked with its pattern chip. Re-etching or removing an etch could come later as a paid option. |
| 2026-10-21 | **Polish shows on etched swords as a sheen** | Allen: "the look also stays the same. is this intended?" The etch covered the whole face, hiding the polish. Of three options (a sheen, a bare cutting edge, a see-through etch on the bench), Allen chose "Etch gets a sheen". The etch's dark body brightens toward a cool sheen at each polish level (0, ½, 1), on the bench and in hand, and the patterns are unchanged. See [[Art/Animated Blade Etching]]. |
| 2026-10-22 | **A broader town street is built beside the current one, to compare** | Allen: "build it parallel to our existing map so I can compare". From the Town Street Redesign pack, with Claude's first-pass numbers: fronts 72 apart (current 44), a market widening of 112 at lots N2/S2, two staggered side lanes, same lots and front cross-section, so the plot template fits unchanged. `Workspace.Village` stays the live street until Allen picks. | [[Art/Town Street Redesign References#Built for comparison (2026-10-22)]], [[Sessions/2026-10-22]] |
| 2026-10-22 | **Calmer fronts in the redesigned street** | Allen asked whether the map was too busy. Claude: the street is close to empty, but the fronts are busier than the references. Allen: "try that out." Wider houses in runs of one style, storehouses and gable fronts only at corners and the market, fewer signs and lanterns on plain townhouses, tile roofs only, generated flagstone paving. v1 is kept in Legacy. | [[Art/Town Street Redesign References#Calmer fronts (v2, 2026-10-22)]], [[Sessions/2026-10-22]] |
| 2026-10-22 | **A simplified six-plot map is built as a third candidate** | Allen: "could we also try a simplified map", from the new brief, image 06 and its prompt. Six identical plots separated by 24-wide paved lanes on a 72-wide flagstone street, a modest junction at the middle pair, two staggered lane exits, a walled town with gate and small shrine, six small edge houses and no clutter. `Workspace.Village` stays live until Allen picks among the current street, the redesign and this. | [[Art/Town Street Redesign References#Simplified map built (2026-10-22)]], [[Sessions/2026-10-22]] |

The six-plot walled town with paved lanes between the plots replaces the continuous town street. The old street and the redesign are parked in `ServerStorage.Legacy`; unclaimed lots keep closed shopfronts. Supersedes "The map is a Japanese town street" (2026-10-21) for the layout; the architecture and style carry over. | [[Art/Town Street Redesign References#Simplified map built (2026-10-22)]], [[Sessions/2026-10-22]] |

Supersedes the closed shopfronts of 2026-10-21. | [[Design/Six Plots]], [[Sessions/2026-10-22]] |
| 2026-10-22 | **Plots a full shop width apart** | Allen: "Also expand the map and separate the houses more". | 2026-10-22 | **Gable-front roofs** | Allen: "Also can we try to rotate the roofs so the sides so looking at it front on, the slant is left right". Replaces the eaves-to-the-street roofs of 2026-10-21. | [[Archive/Project History/Code Map before cleanup — 2026-10-22#Simplified map (2026-10-22)]], [[Sessions/2026-10-22]] |

| 2026-10-22 | **Workshop interior laid out as one working room** | Allen's request from a screenshot of the old layout. Supply chest back-left (replaces the alcove), furnaces freestanding on a shared hearth, the anvil centred in front of them on a stump and floor stone, a finishing bench along the right wall in matching sections (hilt → polish → etch toward the door, each section still unlocking with its station), racks kept, a few tool rails and shelves. Station names, prompts, mounts and mechanics unchanged; the hilt bench's view now turns with the bench. | [[Art/Workshop Layout]], [[Sessions/2026-10-22]] |
| 2026-10-22 | **The ore roller is a loot box; collect before rolling again** | Allen: "try to make our ore roller look like a Call of Duty loot box, where you open it, the ore floats around and spins its different types, and then falls down onto the lootbox again"; "players need to pick up their ores before rolling again". The supply chest becomes a strapped box whose lid holds all six slots. A pull opens it: the ingots float up, spin through the ore types, lock in and drop onto the shut lid. The lever is off while rolled ore waits. Supersedes the unlimited re-rolls of 2026-10-20. | [[Art/Ore Loot Box]], [[Sessions/2026-10-22]] |
| 2026-10-22 | **The first sword goes quicker** | Allen: "first time tutorial, the furnace and other processes need to be sped up". Claude's defaults: heating is the approved 10 s, and 3 s until the player's first sword is finished; until the first sale, listing a sword brings a customer at once. The roll, anvil and hilt are active steps and keep their timing. | [[Archive/Project History/Code Map before cleanup — 2026-10-22#First sword and delivery bay (2026-10-22)]], [[Sessions/2026-10-22]] |
| 2026-10-22 | **A delivery bay beside every shop** | Allen: "each plot should have an area where we can eventually add a delivery carrier. maybe a guy with a carriage. potentially could carry over into the street." Space only until the delivery board. | [[Archive/Project History/Code Map before cleanup — 2026-10-22#First sword and delivery bay (2026-10-22)]], [[Sessions/2026-10-22]] |
| 2026-10-22 | **A door to the delivery bay, hidden until delivery is unlocked** | Allen asked: "When delivery is unlocked, is there a way to have a door to the delivery bay?", then, to build it hidden: "Until delivery is unlocked." The shop's left wall panel beside the bay is swapped for a doorway (sliding door, noren) when an upgrade with id `Delivery` is bought; both shops have it. Price, pad and place in the chain come with the delivery board. | [[Archive/Project History/Code Map before cleanup — 2026-10-22#First sword and delivery bay (2026-10-22)]], [[Sessions/2026-10-22]] |
| 2026-10-22 | **Fair first offer built; haggling taught on the second sale** | The approved tutorial rule (2026-10-19), built. Claude's defaults: the fair offer is exactly the sword's value; declining it gets no counter-offer (no haggling on the first sale); on the second sale the card says "Decline and they may offer more" and the first decline always brings a better offer, then the usual rules apply. | [[Archive/Project History/Code Map before cleanup — 2026-10-22#Fair first offer (2026-10-22)]], [[Sessions/2026-10-22]] |
| 2026-10-22 | **Five more etching patterns: Tide, Stormroot, Woven Wind, Fracture, Falling Stars** | Allen asked for the concept sheet's five to be built, free for now. Claude's defaults: each is +15% like Flame and Eclipse, with Eclipse's dark body and fallback mark colour, and marks in the material's colour; no new guards. Every pattern draws in the same 3.0 s, so the stylus is faster on long ones. Tide keeps at least two crests (a tantō gets two). Woven Wind twists once per about 7 blade widths (3 / 2 / 1). Falling Stars puts a star per about 6.5 widths (3 / 2 / 1). Fracture is filled shards revealed behind a scribbling stylus (not outlines), shrinking from panels to diamonds toward the tip, with an upright fault on each texture-strip seam. | [[Art/Animated Blade Etching#Five more patterns (2026-10-22)]], [[Sessions/2026-10-22]] |
| 2026-10-22 | **First-session guide: one line and an arrow** | Allen asked for roadmap items 1–3. Claude's defaults, from the pottery review's "what and where": a short line at the top names the next action and a bouncing amber arrow marks the station. It reads the player's own state, so it resumes after respawns and rejoins. No line while an ingot heats (the furnace shows the time), nothing while a station camera or picker is up, and nothing once the lessons are done. | [[Development/Code Map#First session and saving (2026-10-22)]], [[Sessions/2026-10-22#Roadmap 1–3: build audit, first-session journey and saving]] |
| 2026-10-22 | **Teaching commissions: visitors, the request board and rewards** | Claude's defaults for the approved sequence. The **Guard Captain** walks in after the first sale and asks for the Iron Wakizashi (90%+, **120 coins**; an Iron Wakizashi sells for about 95). E on him or on the shop's request board accepts and teaches the wakizashi; the sword is handed in at the board through a picker. The **Wandering Swordsman** asks for a Katana of any metal (90%+, **300 coins**) after the wakizashi is delivered and the third shop sale, and teaches it. Requests don't expire: an unaccepted one waits on the board and its visitor comes back next visit. No guaranteed Iron (still the open proposal in [[Archive/Design/Core Loop Review]]). Rewards and triggers are tuning knobs. | [[Development/Code Map#First session and saving (2026-10-22)]], [[Sessions/2026-10-22#Roadmap 1–3: build audit, first-session journey and saving]] |
| 2026-10-22 | **Blueprints are learned; fittings carry a tradition** | Only learned blueprints show at the anvil and can be forged (checked by the server): the tantō from the start, the others when their commission is accepted. Every blueprint and hilt has a tradition, all Japanese for now; the bench only offers, and the server only fits, hilts of the blade's tradition. Fitting milestones stay 0/2/4/7/10/15 swords until playtesting. | [[Development/Code Map#First session and saving (2026-10-22)]] |
| 2026-10-22 | **Saving: what comes back and how** | Claude's defaults for roadmap 3: session-locked DataStore saves (contents in [[Development/Saving]]). Displayed swords go back on the same holders, rack contents on the racks, upgrades on whichever lot the player gets. An ingot left in a furnace comes back hot; ore rolled but not collected comes back as cold ingots; a blade mid-forge restarts from its still-hot ingot. A lock still in use is waited on for about 30 s, then taken over. A failed load kicks with a rejoin message rather than starting blank. Studio saves to its own store. | [[Development/Saving]], [[Sessions/2026-10-22#Roadmap 1–3: build audit, first-session journey and saving]] |
| 2026-10-22 | **Employees: visible work, one user per station, station-based unlocks** | Allen's inbox notes, answered: employees play the crafting animation at their station; a station is kept by whoever starts a job there (player or employee) and the other waits; each employee unlocks when its role's second station is bought (furnace operator after Furnace 2, smith after Anvil 2), and the cashier with the Upgraded Store. Design only; employees are roadmap 5. | [[Design/Workshop Growth#Station sharing, unlocks and visible work (confirmed 2026-10-22)]] |
| 2026-10-22 | **Offers come from the customer, shown as a percentage** | Allen: "the accept/decline of a sale should be a popup from the NPC"; the price "does not need to be shown ... $100 [+20%]". The offer is a bubble over the customer (owner only), the 20 s timer runs wherever you are; it reads "$120 [+20%]" in green above the sword's worth, "$85 [-15%]" in red below, "$100" when equal. Displayed swords have no tag. Claude's defaults: "$" wording, the bubble titled with the sword name. | [[Sessions/2026-10-22#Inbox items resolved]] |
| 2026-10-22 | **The first request takes any metal** | Allen: the tutorial "may take many rolls" to find Iron; he chose any metal over a supplied ingot or a guaranteed roll. The Guard Captain asks for a Wakizashi (90%+, 120 coins); the id stays `IronWakizashi` for saves. Resolves Core Loop Review refinement 2. | [[Sessions/2026-10-22#Inbox items resolved]] |
| 2026-10-22 | **Hilt seated by dragging** | Allen: three clicks "doesn't seem very interactive". Drag the hilt along onto the tang in one motion (or hold Space / A / R2); it seats with a clang. Untimed, can't fail; the seat bonus is unchanged. | [[Sessions/2026-10-22#Inbox items resolved]] |
| 2026-10-22 | **"Workshop open!" after Ore Slot 2** | Allen: after Ore Slot 2 "no indication of whats next". A short banner and confetti when it's bought after the first request. | [[Sessions/2026-10-22#Inbox items resolved]] |
| 2026-10-22 | **Gems, daily streak and the Gem Shop** | Allen: daily rewards "should give gems ... used to buy shop upgrades you can't get in the physical shop". Gems are saved; a daily 7-day streak that never resets gives 5, 5, 10, 10, 15, 15, 30; the Gem Shop (button on the left) sells Better Advertising (25/60/120: walk-ins 12–22 → 6–12 s) and More Clients (40/100: 2 → 4 at once). VIP clients later. Prices and amounts are Claude's defaults. | [[Sessions/2026-10-22#Inbox items resolved]] |
| 2026-10-22 | **Villagers, NPC collisions, markers, hotbar gap, anvil chart** | From Allen's inbox: 5 villagers stroll the street's centre; customers and villagers don't collide with players; the + markers sit centred on each holder, unclipped, within 9 studs; the hotbar has a clear gap; the anvil chart is redrawn so both outlines share one frame and read as the sword. | [[Sessions/2026-10-22#Inbox items resolved]] |
| 2026-10-22 | **Gamepad controls for the core loop** | From the build audit: R2 strikes and B leaves the anvil; Y buys on an upgrade pad; Y accepts and L2 declines an offer card (the buttons say "(Y)" and "(L2)" on a gamepad; X stays the prompts' key, and a pad's Buy (Y) wins over the card while you stand on it); R1/L1 cycle the hotbar through the bar and then the bag. Polishing still has no gamepad input. | [[Sessions/2026-10-22#Roadmap 1–3: build audit, first-session journey and saving]] |
