## Current open design work

Consolidated with live numbers and recommendations on 2026-10-23: [[Archive/Design/Alignment Review — 2026-10-23#Decisions waiting]].

- Review [[Archive/Design/Core Loop Review|Core Loop Review]]: first-session flow, picker clarity, heating pace, sales interruptions, and polishing incentive.
- Tune circle count, polishing duration/feedback, Japanese blueprint prices, fittings milestones, and visitor unlocks.
- Define the letter interface, storage, and exact customer taste matching. Commission delivery is through a sword picker.
- Choose specific special characters and their requests.
- Tune the five initial employee roles and later ore attendant: starting quality, levels, speed, finisher polish ceilings, hiring and worker-count limits; reconcile the older 90% worker/recovery model with 100% player forging and the separate polish bonus; define data/value rules before implementation. Hilting and polishing share the finisher role. Define paid recipe-training scope, ownership and costs.
- ~~Define courier order rules, timers, capacity, rewards and persistence~~ — decided and built 2026-10-22 ([[#Deliveries, bright shop, nameplate, icons — 2026-10-22]]); tune the numbers after play.
- Tune weather traffic bonuses/scheduling and the implemented roof fade/cutaway transitions. Pottery comparison values remain unapproved balance.

## Roadmap brainstorming decisions — 2026-10-22

- **Wealth through relaxed swordmaking:** Allen chose sword discovery/crafting as the activity focus, relaxed successful crafting over precision-based quality, and becoming rich as the larger tycoon fantasy.
- **Permanent growth:** retain wealth and workshop progress, expanding through valuable swords, facilities and prestigious customers; no rebirth/prestige reset in the current plan. Late-game milestones and expansion limits remain open.
- **Value first, then scale:** personally make more valuable swords, automate familiar production, then personally pursue new designs and special commissions.
- **Mixed unlocks:** profits buy most upgrades and blueprints; milestone commissions introduce special designs and new sword traditions.
- **Paid training per sword shape:** personally craft the shape before spending coins to train employees in it. Training covers any permitted supplied material, not separate material-and-shape combinations. Ownership, prices, material permissions and finish choices remain undecided.
- **Trained smiths can match manual quality:** lower starter output progresses toward **100% forging quality** when fully trained, replacing the older 90% employee ceiling. The player's continuing focus is new discoveries and special commissions. Starting quality, curve/costs and recovery mechanics remain open; finisher polishing capability is separate.
- **No ongoing wages:** hiring, recipe training and worker upgrades are one-time purchases; idle employees do not drain coins.
- **Later capped offline earnings:** once online automation is established and balanced, permit limited workshop earnings while away, collected on return. Cap duration, rate, unlock, stock consumption and ore-attendant contribution remain open. This supersedes leaving offline earnings as an unselected inbox idea; courier offline behavior is separate.
- **Staged ore supply:** player-rolled and stocked ore initially; an ore attendant is a later upgrade using the existing roller and material odds. This supersedes the older exclusion of an ore attendant and extends the five initial professions with a later sixth.
- **First employee revised — sword crafter:** Allen selected a worker who operates the anvil, polishes and puts the sword in its rack. This supersedes the first-hire smith/finisher separation and makes the earlier five-role count provisional. Hilt assembly responsibility and input supply need clarification before finalizing the combined job; polishing remains on assembled swords.

These are design decisions, not implementation claims. See [[Design/Workshop Growth#Wealth and staged automation (confirmed 2026-10-22)]] and [[Development/Roadmap]]. Claude is working on roadmap sections 1–3; this discussion refines later progression.

## Core loop additions — 2026-10-22

Allen picked rotating requests, reputation, Faster Heating / Lucky Roller, a coins/gems HUD, a sword complete card and NEW metal discovery. Claude's defaults, all tuning knobs ([[Sessions/2026-10-22#Core loop: requests, reputation, heat/luck, HUD, reward card]]):

- **Orders open after the first commission** (the Iron Wakizashi hand-in), 2 on the board, 3 from reputation 3. A twist's station must be bought before it is asked for. Reward ≈ 1.5× the least fitting sword's shop value, +5% per reputation level, plus 1–3 gems. No expiry and no skip for now.
- **Reputation never drops** (cozy: no penalty for declining offers). Points 1 / 3 / 5 for sale / order / commission; 8 levels. Effects now: walk-ins and the third order; special visitors later.
- **Faster Heating and Lucky Roller have three tiers each** (the GDD listed Lucky Roller I–V); luck multiplies all non-Common odds and Common takes the rest.
- **The HUD shows coins and reputation beside the gem button; the reward card waits for station cameras to close** and shows the gain from the step just done (not a value breakdown).

## The sword crafter — 2026-10-23

Allen's answers: the crafter fits the best hilt you've unlocked; takes cold ingots from the Ingot Rack and heats them at a free furnace; starts at 70% and levels to 100%. Claude's defaults, all tuning knobs: Anvil 2 1,500 after the Ingot Rack; hire 1,000; training tantō 200 / wakizashi 600 / katana 1,500, only for shapes you've made; +3% a level, a level every 3 swords; forge 30 → 15 s, polish 10 → 5 s; cheapest ingot first; it always polishes fully when the station is bought and free; it fits hilts at Anvil 2, not the Hilt Bench; its swords don't count toward hilt milestones; below 80% the value multiplier floors at ×1.0. Resolves the crafter's open hilt and input questions in [[Design/Workshop Growth#First employee — sword crafter (latest decision, 2026-10-22)]]. [[Sessions/2026-10-23#The sword crafter (first employee)]]

## Strong-glow etchings — 2026-10-23

Allen picked the strongest of three rendered looks: thicker strokes, whitened cores, a wide coloured halo on the dark etch body, so every metal's marks read (Blue Steel, Obsidian and Starmetal included). Accepted cost: about 2.3× the build time, built over frames on world swords; colours sit closer together. [[Sessions/2026-10-23#Etchings that pop (strong glow)]]

## Deliveries, bright shop, nameplate, icons — 2026-10-22

- **Deliveries follow Create Pottery!'s window** (Allen's two screenshots): Orders and Cart tabs, Bulk and Special orders with Skip, Capacity and Speed bought in the window (not on pads), the four-step trip tracker, the order pinned on screen. Claude's numbers, all tuning knobs: Delivery Cart 1,200 coins after Ore Slot 2; capacity 3–6 crates; trips 2:30 → 1:10; pay ≈ request pay × count × 1.1, one reputation per sword. One order at a time; offers don't expire; cancel before sending returns the swords; trips finish offline (server clock). Courier capacity is the order size limit. Supersedes the open delivery rules in [[Design/Workshop Growth#Optional remote delivery orders]].
- **Bright shop:** roofs cast no shadow into the shop, a pale floor and soft unshadowed lamps; toned down once at Allen's request.
- **Plot nameplate:** avatar bust and "<name>'s Plot" high over every claimed plot.
- **Icons come from VectorIcons' decals for now** ([[Art/Icon Library]]).

## Economy pass 1 — 2026-10-23

Allen took decisions 1–3 of [[Archive/Design/Alignment Review — 2026-10-23#Decisions waiting]] as recommended ([[Sessions/2026-10-23#Economy pass 1]]):

- **One new board order every ~3 minutes** while the board has room, instead of an instant refill. Claude's defaults: the board still opens full after the first commission; the clock runs on the server clock and is saved, so the board fills while you're away; a new reputation slot waits its turn; a dimmed "New order · in m:ss" card shows when the next one comes.
- **Orders pay 1.2× the handed-in sword's value; deliveries 1.3× the packed swords' value.** Supersedes the flat estimates (about 1.5× a middling sword plus 5% a reputation level; for deliveries that × count × 1.1). Claude's defaults: the board card says "+20% value" and the picker shows each sword's pay; a delivery card shows "+30%" until something is packed, then the coins.
- **Polish multiplies the whole value by 1.10.** Supersedes "+10% value" added to the other finish bonuses (2026-10-20). The "+10% value" texts stay, now exact.
- **The cart pays 1.1× the packed swords' value** (decision 20, the same day). At 1.3×, with only the trip as a limit, the cart beat the shop's average first offer for every sword; 1.1× sits above a first offer and below a patient or liked sale. Supersedes the 1.3× above. [[Sessions/2026-10-23#Cart pay lowered to 1.1×]]

## Refinements 2 — 2026-10-23

Allen's [[Refinements2]] notes and two additions made during the work ([[Sessions/2026-10-23#Refinements 2]]):

- **A HUD menu of icon buttons** (VectorIcons): Shop, Charts for the store upgrades "for now" (replacing the gem icon on that button), a gift for the daily reward, a sword for the collection book (replacing the book), a crew button once an employee is hired (Friends icon) and Settings. Claude's defaults: a 3-across grid under the coin, reputation and gem counters; gems become a counter like coins.
- **The Shop button opens a Robux shop, empty for now** (Allen picked it over pointing it at the Gem Shop): the window says "Coming soon" until gem packs or passes exist.
- **Settings: Music and Sound on/off, saved with progress** (Allen's pick over volume sliders or an empty window).
- **Hiring the crafter is a tycoon pad** ("Buy Worker … instead of opening GUI"): Sword Crafter, 1,000 coins, after Anvil 2, on Anvil 2's spot. Supersedes the window's Hire card.
- **Two more display tables in the Upgraded Store:** Claude's prices 900 and 1,300, chained after Display Table 2, mirroring Tables 1 and 2 on the right side.
- **The delivery door is there from the start, shut**, and the Delivery Cart slides it open; the pad keeps its timing (after Ore Slot 2).
- **The finishing stations stand apart** (3 studs between counters), and the etching wall shows three real etched swords instead of a part-built prop.
- **Studio's Reset progress starts over in place** instead of kicking; the Dev panel sits bottom right and folds away.
- **No "Take back" label** on a holder with your sword; pads sink in when stepped on.

## Refinements 2, second list — 2026-10-23

Allen rewrote [[Refinements2]] with a new list and answered questions on it ([[Sessions/2026-10-23#Refinements 2, second list]]):

- **Requests come by mail; no visitors, no request board.** "remove the concept of the visitors with their requests. i want the requests to come through mail. for example, we can have a mail button. when clicked it opens your inbox with mail from various people that wanted something. when you have what they're looking for, you can submit in the UI to them and get your reward." Allen took the recommended follow-ups: **every order is mail too** (the request board is removed; same 3-minute clock, pay and gems) and the tutorial goes **letter, then pad** (after the first sale the Guard Captain's letter arrives; the guide says open your mail, then buy the Wakizashi Blueprint, then hand it in from the mail, then Ore Slot 2). Supersedes the in-person teaching commissions and the board (2026-10-19, 2026-10-22).
- **Blueprints are tycoon pads:** "the different swords you get are earned by just buying them through the tycoon pads." Wakizashi Blueprint 60, Katana Blueprint 400 after it (Allen picked 60 / 400 over 150 / 1,000). Claude's defaults: one pad spot by the anvil; letters no longer teach; a blueprint learned from an older commission counts its pad as bought; Studio's Skip tutorial no longer teaches blueprints.
- **Crew window: the full Create Pottery! set** (roster, stats, station page), with **one crafter working at Anvil 2** (Allen picked it over adding Anvils 3–4). Taise is the first crafter's name "for now"; an overhead role, name and level in that order; no "Need ingots" bubble (it stands idle); slow walking; the hammer held right.
- **The ore roller moves to the left wall, by the Ingot Rack** (out of the back corner).
- **Station windows hide the rest of the HUD** and bring it back when closed.
- **Workshop racks use the shop holders' icon markers.**
- **Someone pulls the delivery cart**, out and back, seen coming home as the timer ends.

## More refinements — 2026-10-23

Allen's [[More refinements]] notes and answers ([[Sessions/2026-10-23#More refinements]]):

- **A station the crafter is using, or heading to, is blocked for you:** "an employee is using something, we shouldnt be able to use it. also if employee is on the way to sue something, it should also be blocked." Claude's defaults: covers the Ingot Rack, furnaces, the Polishing Station and the Finished Sword Rack; the crafter only takes a furnace with nothing heating in it; it gives the full sword rack back while waiting for room.
- **The cart is the quick, sure sale; the shop pays more:** "delivery means you get guarantee cash and its quicker to dump a bunch of swords into it to get money quick, but store means people are going to usually pay more." Allen picked **0.8× the swords' value** (over 0.9× and 1.0×) and **a bigger cart where a Bulk Order takes any sword** (over bigger with named shapes, or sizes unchanged). Claude's defaults: Capacity 6 / 9 / 12 / 15, Special Orders 2–4 swords, bulk gems 1 + count ÷ 3. Supersedes the 1.1× above.
- **Parking bays, as rickshaws:** "in the clay game, there's also a concept of parking spaces. upgrading parking space means more cars can pull in and means more customers." Allen picked **rickshaws** (over horses at hitching posts and ox carts) and **keeping More Clients, the bays adding to it** (over replacing it). Claude's defaults: three bays on the street in front of the shop, Rickshaw Bay 1 (500, after Display Table 1) → 2 (1,500) → 3 (4,000); customers beyond More Clients' count come by rickshaw.
- **NPCs walk at 6** ("in the clay game, npcs have a walk speed of 6"): customers and villagers. Claude's default: the crafters keep their roster walk speeds.
- **Ingots on the shelf look like ingots:** a bevelled trapezoid bar mesh, also used for the carried ingot and picker cards.
- **"Katana Sword", not "Katana Blueprint"**, on the pads (and "Wakizashi Sword").

## Urgency phase 1 — 2026-10-23

Allen asked for stage 1 of [[Archive/Design/Workshop Urgency Recommendations]] with Claude making the implementation choices ([[Sessions/2026-10-23#Urgency phase 1: cues, crew status, 45 s offers]]):

- **Regular offers last 45 s** (was 20), as the brief's starting value; prices and haggling stay on the card at the customer. Playtest: an anvil visit plus the walk to the shop takes about 35 s, so 45 s fits it; hand polishing is unmeasured.
- **Four quiet cue sounds, each with a visual twin:** furnace ready (ding; the ingot glows and "Ready!" shows through walls), a new customer's offer (one bell; a coin and the seconds left over a customer you're too far from, no price), a sale (coins; the existing notice and "+N"), the cart back (a rattle; the pinned order's green "Ready!"). In `SoundService.Effects.Cues`, muted by Settings' Sound switch, one per 1.5 s per kind; none for the crafter's own steps or a same-customer raise. Claude's picks from the ProSoundEffects library, unheard; Allen to judge by ear.
- **A waiting customer's card comes 1.5 s after the last one closed** (playtest: it replaced the old card in the same instant).
- **The crew window explains the crafter:** what it is doing, or why it waits (no shape, no sword rack, rack full, no ingots, no free furnace, you polishing), on its roster card and Stats panel, amber while waiting, with a dot on the HUD's crew button. Keeps the no-speech-bubbles decision (Refinements2).
- **Overlapping work is taught once, after the tutorial** (Ore Slot 2 bought): "Heat the next ingot first" while you hold a hot ingot or a bare blade with a cold ingot and a free furnace, until you do it. Not saved (can return once a session, never after 10 swords).

## Urgency phase 2 — 2026-10-23

Allen asked for the recommended next changes; Claude built stage 2 of [[Archive/Design/Workshop Urgency Recommendations]] and made these choices (all easy to undo; not played) ([[Sessions/2026-10-23#Urgency phase 2: the crafter's furnace and your forgotten ingot]]):

- **Anvil 2 needs the Ingot Rack, Furnace 2 and the Finished Sword Rack** (was the Ingot Rack only), so a working crafter always has its own furnace, input and output. Plots that already own Anvil 2 or the crafter keep them.
- **A ready ingot you leave in a furnace for 10 s may be taken out by the crafter when it needs that furnace;** it goes into your hands, still hot, with one notice. Chosen over reserving a furnace for the crafter (that would leave you one) and over waiting (what phase 1 found blocked it most).
- **Buffers unchanged** until play shows a need (rack 6 ≈ 7 min of crafter output, Ingot Rack 12 ≈ 14 min; the Ingot Rack became 6 later the same day at Allen's request, about 7 min). **Protecting special metals from the crafter** stays open until it's designed; the crafter still takes the cheapest ingot first.

## Racks, ingots, NPC walking — 2026-10-23

Allen's list ([[Sessions/2026-10-23#Racks split, one ingot model, NPC walking, no offer ping]]):

- **Workshop racks: store and take apart.** The + opens only what you can store; clicking or tapping an item on the rack takes it (the bag still opens a take-only list).
- **One ingot model everywhere:** the ore box's ingot, at its size, on the rack, in hand and on cards.
- **Ingot Rack holds 6** full-size ingots (was 12 small ones). Older saves with more keep them.
- **The coin-and-seconds ping over far customers is removed** (reverses that part of urgency phase 1; the bell stays).
- **NPCs path round furniture and walk without stutter** (crafter everywhere, customers inside the shop).
- **A marker per rack slot** (later the same day, Allen: "it should be 6 to hold the 6 ingots"): bags take, + stores; and the crafter polishes standing, arm out, not lunging ([[Sessions/2026-10-23#Ingot grip, a marker per rack slot, polishing pose]]).
- **Station pickers show only swords you can use there** (Allen: "they should just not be be shown"): polished swords at polishing, etched at etching, and non-fitting swords for deliveries and mail are left out, not greyed ([[Sessions/2026-10-23#Pickers show only swords you can use]]).

## Sword-shaping experiment — 2026-10-23

An isolated prototype ([[Prototypes/sword-shaping/README]], [[Sessions/2026-10-23#Sword-shaping prototype (experiment)]]) to test whether choosing where to hammer, turning the blade and correcting its shape is worth doing by hand for rare materials. **Not approved as a replacement for current forging**; whether to keep exploring it is open.

- **Rules being tested:** edge up narrows, flat widens; the player chooses any spot; no prescribed strike order; overshoot allowed; length and curve fixed for now.
- **Score:** Shape match from the finished shape only, separate from quality and value. Allen: "any way we can be more generous about the accuracy matching?" → within **0.08 cm** of the line counts as perfect, which puts 100% in reach with full strikes. Craftsmanship bonuses and crafter ceilings stay later decisions.

## Metal textures — 2026-10-23

Allen: "Im looking for a way we can either generate custom materials for our game or use free online assets. That way we're not just using a solid color for all of our ingots/swords", then "Try" ([[Sessions/2026-10-23#Metal textures on ingots and blades]]).

- **One shared, tinted surface instead of one texture per metal:** near-white maps that the metal's colour tints, as the Genshin-style Paint variants do. Ingots are hammered; blades go forged → brushed → mirror with their polish (the existing BladeShine levels).
- **Generated with numpy, not Material Maker:** the Material Maker MCP needs Godot 4.7 and a Material Maker checkout and is alpha; it is kept in mind for the few metals that want their own pattern (Damascus, Starmetal, Rainbow Alloy). Free CC0 sets (ambientCG, Poly Haven) are an option too but look realistic.

## New metals and a Secret tier — 2026-10-23

Allen: "Implement some of these" (from the metal brainstorm, [[Design/Metal Ideas]]). Picked by me, one per tier, to try each way of adding a metal ([[Sessions/2026-10-23#New metals]]):

- **Pewter** (Common), **Rose Gold** (Uncommon), **Jade** (Rare, glass like Obsidian): tint only.
- **Dragonsteel** (Legendary): its own scale texture.
- **Watermelon** in a new **Secret** rarity: ×25 value, 0.1% (taken from Common), red rarity colour. Proposed numbers; Allen to confirm. Joke metals go here.
- Adding a metal dilutes the others in its tier (Iron, Copper and Stone each 15% instead of 20%).
- **Etches keep a textured metal visible** (Allen: "Yes ideally the texture stays visible"): on Dragonsteel and Watermelon an etch draws only its band or marks, with no dark body. Dragonsteel's etch colour became gold so it reads on red scales ([[Sessions/2026-10-23#Etches on textured metals]]).
- **30 metals** (Allen: "I want to have around 30 different metals/ingots in the game"): twelve more, picked by me from [[Design/Metal Ideas]] and spread 5 / 6 / 6 / 5 / 5 / 3 over Common to Secret. Cheap looks first (Roblox materials: Glass gems, Ice, Marble, CrackedLava, Neon, Cardboard, Rubber). Each tier's odds stay; its metals share them ([[Sessions/2026-10-23#Thirty metals]]).
- **The collection book shows metals, not swords** (Allen: "instead of every sword combination in the book, i think it makes more sense to just have every material you've discovered. we can just use the ingot"): one card per metal with its ingot as an image, not a 3D viewport; metals not yet collected are dark ingot silhouettes ("they should just be dark or greyed out ingot silhouettes"). The blade × metal records stay in the save, unused ([[Sessions/2026-10-23#Collection book: one ingot per metal]]).

## Blueprint shapes — 2026-10-23

- **New blueprints must look different.** Allen: more Japanese variants (tachi, nodachi, ō-kissaki…) are "a bit redundant, they look very similar". Next blueprints are picked for clearly different outlines (length class, width profile, curve, point), not more katana-like Japanese blades. Which ones, and in what order, is still open: [[Design/Sword Profile Ideas#Suggested order]]. ([[Sessions/2026-10-23#More sword shapes]])
- **Three western swords: longsword, falchion, gladius** (Allen: "Lets do 3 western swords, the longsword, falchion, and the gladius, along with their respective hilts"). They're bought on the blueprint pad after the Katana Sword: Longsword 1,000 → Falchion 2,000 → Gladius 3,500 coins, with base prices 160 / 220 / 280. Prices, stars and order are my placeholders until the economy pass. Each has its own hilt model ([[Sessions/2026-10-23#Western swords and automatic hilts]]).
- **Hilts are fitted automatically, not chosen** (Allen: "instead of having players choose their hilts, when they go to the hilt station, the correct hilt is automatically applied.. so japanese swords have a japanese hilt, and western swords have their respective hilts. no need to choose"). The blueprint decides the hilt's shape: the katana hilt for Japanese blades, each western blade its own. The six tiers (Wooden … Gem, unlocked at 0/2/4/7/10/15 swords, +0 to +40%) stay and only colour it; the bench always fits the best tier unlocked. This supersedes "Fittings match each sword tradition … choose fittings" (2026-10-19) and the tradition check from 2026-10-22.
- **Every etch is see-through** (Allen: "lets do the see through etch on every metal like we do with dragonsteel and watermlon"): an etch draws only its band or marks, in the metal's etch colour, over the blade's own metal; no dark body on any metal. Swords etched before are drawn the same way. The etched guard keeps a dark iron centre ([[Sessions/2026-10-23#See-through etch on every metal]]).

## Furnace model — 2026-10-23

- **Allen's smelting furnace replaces the old furnace model** (Allen: "can we replace our current furnace with this"), for both furnaces, at the old furnace's height. The door's vents glow while an ingot heats ([[Sessions/2026-10-23#Smelting furnace replaces the furnace model]]).

## Sword slashes — 2026-10-23

- **Clicking with a held sword swings a slash combo, looks only** (Allen: "players can click and it does slashes. it doesn't need to do damage, just the animations"). It's a four-slash combo, one slash per click (Allen first asked for three: "clicking once would play the first slash, 2nd time, 2nd slash, and 3rd time for final slash"; then, after a recording, "maybe we can separate into 4 different slashes?"), cut from the `Sword Slashes` combo in `Workspace["Sword Animations"]`. Wait too long between clicks and it starts over. There's no hit detection or damage. Combat stays out of scope ([[Sessions/2026-10-23#Sword slashes on click]]).

## Big swords — 2026-10-23

- **Big swords are a category** (Allen: "the big swords would require you to purchase a standing big sword rack and it only holds one. they can sell for a lot"). The first is the **Zanbatō**, 100 cm (Allen: "Shrink the Zanbatō blade to about 100 cm so it's around 6.5 studs with the grip").
- **Claude's defaults, to tune:** the Big Sword Stand costs 2,500 and comes after the Upgraded Store; it stands by the back wall, left of the workshop door; the sword stands upright, blade up. The Zanbatō is ★5 at 1,000 coins base with the katana's hilt for now, and it has no blueprint pad yet. Big swords go only on the stand and nothing else goes there ([[Sessions/2026-10-23#Big Sword Stand]]).
- **The Zanbatō's point is on the back** (Allen chose "Slanted tip reversed"): the back runs straight to the point and the chisel cut slopes down to the edge, as in the first buster-sword reference ([[Sessions/2026-10-23#Zanbatō tip reversed]]).

## Refinements 3 — 2026-10-24

From [[Refinements3]] ([[Sessions/2026-10-24#Refinements 3]]).

- **Merge Ore Slots, a rebirth for the ore box only** (Allen: "once you get 6 rollers, you can actually merge so that rare clay turns up more often and you get back a single roller and new ones cost more. it goes from 1x to 1.5x. Its basically a rebirth"; then "Repeatable, gentler prices"). Free, repeatable: Ore Slots 2–6 reset, luck ×(1 + 0.5 per merge) on top of Lucky Roller, Ore Slot prices ×1.5 per merge. Coins, upgrades and everything else stay. This is the one exception to "no rebirth/prestige reset" (2026-10-22), which otherwise stands.
- **Luck keeps paying past the old ceiling** (Claude): the rarer tiers take their luck-multiplied odds from the rarest down while every tier below keeps at least 10%, so each merge still raises rare ore. Unchanged below luck ~2.24.
- **Spin the ore box as often as you like** (Allen: "Players should be able to spin the ores as many times as they want"). Supersedes "collect before rolling again" (2026-10-22). Ore still waiting goes into your hands first; what doesn't fit is rolled over.
- **Deliveries: you choose the swords** (Allen: "once i accept a delivery. it should show a page that i can then choose which swords to fill into the delivery. look at the clay game reference"): a packing page after Accept, with Auto-Fill (cheapest first), Cancel Order and Fill the Crate. Swords leave your inventory only when the crate is filled.
- **Reputation shows only in the Deliveries window** (Allen chose "Delivery window only"): no star/level pill on the HUD. **The pinned order is off by default**, with a Pin toggle (Allen: "just have it in the delivery window, not visible by default").
- **Mail orders arrive 3 to 10 minutes apart at random, with no countdown** (Allen: "Should be no 'new order in 3 minutes' text. The orders should actually be randomized, with a minimum of 3 minutes to say 10 minutes"). Supersedes "one new order every 3 minutes" (2026-10-23).
- **Left HUD: just the icons**, no plates (Allen: "Remove the background on left UIs so its just the icons"); **the gem shop button is the House icon**.
- **Fewer words:** "Sold X for Y" with no buyer's name; no "(Polished)" in sword names; no "back in your inventory" (and no "on display", same rule); toolbox swords say the blade ("Katana") with the metal small underneath (Allen chose "Metal, small"); no "1x".
- **A held cold ingot goes straight into the furnace**; closing the anvil's picker leaves the anvil.
- **A second Finished Sword Rack** (Allen chose "Second rack" over a bigger one): 600, after the first.
- **New employees, one person per role, each hired on a pad** (Allen: "Implement a Stocker Employee, and cashier employee", "shop promoter … would act as a stat increaser for pulling more people", "sword etcher … in the settings you choose what etch you want him to do. would require a second sword etching station"; then "A pad each"). Supersedes "no automated etcher is selected" (Workshop Growth).
  - **Stocker:** rack → empty shop holders (Allen chose this over also restocking ingots).
  - **Cashier:** sells at or above a % over value that you set on a slider (Allen's rule, quoted in the session note); below it he declines and the customer may raise. Your first two sales stay yours.
  - **Shop Promoter:** walk-ins sooner and one more customer at once (Claude's default for "stat increaser").
  - **Sword Etcher:** works only at Etching Table 2, the pattern picked on his crew page; the Stocker leaves un-etched swords for him.
  - Placeholders to tune (Claude): prices Stocker 2,000 / Cashier 3,000 / Promoter 1,500 / Etching Table 2 2,500 / Etcher 2,000; names Sora, Aki, Kaito, Yume; a level every 5 jobs to Lv. 11, one perk each.
- **Air, a new Epic metal** (Allen: "an airsword that is part transparent?"; then "Epic"): see-through glass blade, solid ingot.
- **Rainbow Alloy gets an original rainbow texture** (Allen: "use the rainbow texture i added"; then "Make my own"): his `rainbow.webp` is a watermarked stock preview, so it isn't used.
- **The middle lots line up with the street** (Allen: "The shop building is misaligned with the actual street"): N2 and S2 no longer stand 8 studs back as a junction.

## Refinement 4 — 2026-10-24

From [[refinement4]] ([[Sessions/2026-10-24#Refinement 4]]).

- **No lowball offers: customers open at the sword's value to +20%** (Allen: "Customers should never make lowball offers"; then "Value to +20%"): Farmer 100–110%, Adventurer 100–115%, Knight 105–120%, ×1.25 when they like it. Supersedes the 70–110% openers (2026-10-19 tuning). Raises and patience are unchanged.
- **Every offer has its own card, over its customer, several at once** (Allen's bug: "A customer says 'I'll give you X coins,' but the corresponding offer cannot be seen"); a customer says the offer only when its card shows. Supersedes "a waiting customer's card comes 1.5 s after the last one closed" (2026-10-23) except for the tutorial's first two sales, which still come one at a time.
- **The Cashier holds the cash** (Allen: "The cashier should hold all the cash so the player can walk up to him and collect it"): his sales go into a till over his counter, collected by walking up to it (Claude's default: no key to press); a save counts it in your coins.
- **The racks are never the crafter's** (Claude, from Allen's bug "Holding an ingot and pressing E sometimes does not store it"): the Ingot Rack and Finished Sword Rack stay usable while he walks over. Narrows "if employee is on the way to use something, it should also be blocked" (More refinements, 2026-10-23) to stations: furnaces, Anvil 2, the Polishing Station.
- **Holding what a rack takes, E stores it** (Claude, as Allen's furnace rule from Refinements 3).
- **The Shop Promoter faces the street and the Cashier the shop** (Allen's bugs: both "facing the wrong way"): the promoter turns only to people in front of him; the cashier doesn't turn.
- **Employees wear catalog outfits** (Allen: "Give employees more complete outfits"; looks chosen by Claude): Japanese work clothes per role, ids in `Shared/Employees`.
- **The furnace corner is dimmer** (Allen: "too bright near the furnace").
- **Ingot Rack 2** (Allen's idea: "one more ingot shelf"; he chose to build both ideas): a second 6-slot shelf, 500, after the Ingot Rack. Price is a placeholder.
- **Smoothing in the shaping prototype** (Allen's idea; he chose to build both ideas): from a 90% match, Smooth files off what still crosses the outline; it never adds material. The 90% is a tuning knob (`ShapingRules.CONFIG.smoothAt`). Still an experiment, not the game's crafting.
- **Any ingot you hold goes on the Ingot Rack, and cools there** (Allen, after playtesting: "ingot is in my hand, pressing the + doesn't put the ingot in … it should open up only when i have nothing selected or say a sword which is ineligible"; his ingot was hot). Supersedes "hot ingots stay in hand" (2026-10-20). **A rack item goes into the slot you click** ("it doesn't put it where i selected"), and keeps it.
- **The Cashier holds the cash; E at him collects it** (Allen: "he should be holding it, and displaying a cash amount that he has. and i should be able to go up and interact, press e with him to get the cash out. not just automatically"). Supersedes collecting by walking up to the counter (same day).
- **Station wall swords are real game swords** (Allen: "the decorations on the station walls with swords should use real swords from our game. there's no reason to create a fake sword when our game has real swords already"): the Hilt Bench's and Polishing Station's part-built samples are replaced by `buildSword` props, like the Etching Table's. Rule for later dressing: show real swords, never part-built ones.

## Phone playtest — 2026-10-24

From Allen's phone screenshots ([[Sessions/2026-10-24#Phone playtest: HUD, toolbox, windows, station walls]]).

- **Mobile first** (Allen: "from now on I want you to remember to prioritize mobile players. it makes up over 70% of the roblox playerbase, so optimizing for that audience is required"). Every UI and interaction is laid out and checked for a landscape phone first.
- **The HUD is one column under Roblox's top-left buttons**: coins, gems, the menu, the pinned order (Allen: "I want the gold and gems status just about under the official roblox icons"). Roblox's chat window moves to the top right (Claude, so it doesn't cover them).
- **The toolbox is centred on the screen** (Allen: "The toolbox isn't centered correctly"), narrower on a phone so it stays clear of the jump button.
- **A window's X is in its top-right corner** (Allen: "its in the middle and not on the top right of the GUI").
- **No sale notice** (Allen: "Remove the 'sold sword for x coins' notification. its not needed").
- **The Hilt Bench wall shows real hilts of several shapes and fittings; the Polishing wall's sword shimmers** (Allen: "put various hilts up on the wall so it shows well that its hilting station", "have the sword shimmer more"). Real game models only, as for the wall swords.
- **Settings and Daily Reward live in Roblox's top bar** (Allen: "7 icons to the left, which is a bit too much ... move setting to the topbar using this package ... move daily rewards up to the top as well"), through TopbarPlus. **A red circle on Daily Reward while it's claimable** ("i'd also like for daily rewards to have a red circle"), cleared by claiming, not by opening the window.
- **A station's view starts only once you've picked** (Allen: "our camera shouldnt be in the forging view yet with the other guis until we select the ingot or sword"): the anvil's pickers come first, over the room.
- **The six ore slots are six raised stands, not a crate** (Allen: "instead of a create, we could have them on 6 individual stands. also a bit higher, less off the floor"), with plain outlined labels. Supersedes the ore loot box's crate (2026-10-22).
- **No limit on ingots in hand** (Allen: "I see a "your hands are full" when holding 6 ingots. remove this limit"). Supersedes "carrying is capped at 6" (2026-10-20) for ingots: a lever pull or a merge takes all the waiting ore ([[Sessions/2026-10-24#No hand limit, 1.7× ingots, labels that don't overlap]]).
- **The ingot is 1.7× bigger, everywhere it shows** (Allen: "just resize the ingot say 1.70x, so when held, when on the rack, when revealed in the spin, it is more visible"; "why does the furnace need to be changed"). Still one model; the stands and both Ingot Racks are spaced out to fit, the furnace is not.
- **Labels over the ore stands are sized in the world, and the roller's pad labels sit low** (Allen: "the button text is overlapping as well as the rest of the texts on the ingots").
- **The workshop is rearranged from Allen's layout planner** (Allen: "Can we reorganize our stations somehow? i want a way to visualize where to move things", then "try to apply my moves"): furnaces and ingot racks on the left wall, anvils on the back hearth, the finishing stations on the right wall, the ore stands in the middle. Supersedes the 2026-10-22 workshop layout for positions. Further rounds go through the same page ([[Sessions/2026-10-24#Planner moves applied]]).
- **The ingot is 1.275× its first size** (Allen: "we can also make the size of the ingots a bit smaller. like 25% smaller"), down from 1.7× the same day; still one model everywhere.
- **The left column hugs Roblox's top bar on a phone and sits down the middle of the left edge on taller screens** (Allen: "after fixing the ui for mobile, it messed up on the desktop version now"). Narrows "the HUD is one column under Roblox's top-left buttons" (the same day) to phones. **HUD layout lives in StarterGui, not in scripts** (Allen: "you should move them how they are in startergui"): it adapts by its own sizes and constraints ([[Sessions/2026-10-24#The left column back in the middle on desktop, laid out in StarterGui]]).

## Soft-bevelled stations — 2026-10-24

- **The stations get soft bevels like the shop's SwordTable** (Allen: "I would like our current stations to also have those soft bevels"). Claude's defaults: same designs and sizes; every block and cylinder part gets a rounded bevel of 0.2 of its smallest side (at most 0.09 studs), scaled from the table top's measured 0.2-of-thickness chamfer, rounded rather than flat for "soft". The furnaces, anvils, prop swords and hilts, and the workshop fittings stay as they are. [[Sessions/2026-10-24#Soft-bevelled stations]]
- **The shop's wall shelves get the same soft bevels** (Allen: "try the shelves in the shop as well"): Wall Racks 1–4, same rule. The hearth, counter, Big Sword Stand and the shop's other pieces stay as they are (Allen: "Leave it at the stations for now"). [[Sessions/2026-10-24#The shop's wall shelves]]

## Station decorations — 2026-10-24

- **Station decorations hang on a wall; the Etching Tables' go on the right wall** (Allen: "have them hanging on the wall to their right"). Claude's defaults for "omit the unnecessary ones": each Etching Table keeps its sign and its rack of etched swords; the ink shelf, chisel board and scroll go; the Polishing Station loses its cloth rail; the Hilt Bench keeps everything (hilts and tsubas are its identity) and only moves flush to the back wall. [[Sessions/2026-10-24#Station decorations on the walls]]

## Polish sparkle — 2026-10-24

- **A mirror-polished blade's sparkle runs along its curve instead of popping at random** (Allen: "have the sparkle run across the blades curve, so it actually looks likes it shimmering"). Claude's defaults: a wave of 14 sparkles base to tip in 0.5 s along the edge bevel, every 2.5–4.5 s (the `Shimmer` wall katana every 1.2–2 s, bigger, with its light following the wave). [[Sessions/2026-10-24#The polish sparkle runs along the blade]]
- **One sparkle, accelerating, not a wave** (Allen: "just 1 sparle effect and it goes along the blade instead of a trail", "it should get faster/accelerate as it gets towards the tip"): a single sparkle travels the curve in 0.7 s with an ease-in (distance = time²). Supersedes the 14-sparkle wave above. [[Sessions/2026-10-24#The polish sparkle runs along the blade]]
- **The polish shimmer is a soft glare, as on a drawn katana** (Allen shared a clip: "i want to replicate that"): a soft white streak a fifth of the blade long on the edge, blooming, sliding guard to tip with the ease-in, fading in and out, in place of the four-point sparkle. Kept guard-to-tip (the clip's runs toward the hilt). [[Sessions/2026-10-24#The polish sparkle runs along the blade]]

## Refinements 5 — 2026-10-25

- **Anvil 2 is a second anvil, shared with the crafter; he has it first** (Allen: "Pressing E on the 2nd anvil should not open up the Sword Crafters/Employee menu. It should act as a second anvil"; picked "Shared, crafter has priority"). He waits while you're at it, then holds it ("In use") until he's done. The crew window opens only from the Employee button; Etching Table 2 has no prompt. Both anvils face the same way. [[Sessions/2026-10-25#Refinements 5]]
- **Lucky Roller I–III removed; merging is the only luck** (Allen: "So we have a merging system that gives luck, but also an upgrade that gives luck?"; picked "Remove it"). No refunds or save handling: the game has no players yet. Supersedes the Lucky Roller rows of 2026-10-22. [[Sessions/2026-10-25#Refinements 5]]
- **Furnace 2 only after Faster Heating III** (Allen). Anvil 2 still needs Furnace 2. [[Sessions/2026-10-25#Refinements 5]]
- **Employees max at Lv. 5, after several hours of work** (Allen: "level up way too fast and have too many levels"; picked "Several hours"). Claude's defaults: Lv. 2/3/4/5 at 10/30/70/150 swords (≈ 12 min, 40 min, 1.5 h, 3 h), the staff ×2.5 jobs; crafter quality +8%/level (70 → 100%). Supersedes Lv. 11 at 3 swords (5 jobs) a level. [[Sessions/2026-10-25#Refinements 5]]
- **Pad labels show only near you, on one line, and crowded pads moved apart** (Allen: "The games upgrade buttons text is too cluttered"; picked "Both"). Claude's defaults: 14 studs, "Name · price"; the blueprint pad to the workshop's middle stone circle (Allen: "it needs to be in a clearer position"), the Ore Slot pad onto the Lucky Roller's old spot. [[Sessions/2026-10-25#Refinements 5]]
- **The ore roller's lever is big and in the middle** (Allen, after the Create Pottery! lever: "it's thick and not a side note. its in the middle"). Claude's defaults: 1.6× with a thick arm and red knob, front and centre of the roller. [[Sessions/2026-10-25#Refinements 5]]
- **Tutorial: UI steps get a spotlight; a Gem Shop step once you have gems** (Allen). Claude's defaults: the screen dims around the button with a pulsing amber ring; "Open the Gem Shop" (House button), then "Gems buy boosts for your shop that last" over the upgrades, once (saved). [[Sessions/2026-10-25#Refinements 5]]
- **Mail Hand in takes the sword itself, for every order** (Allen; picked "Every order"): the cheapest that fits, no picker. [[Sessions/2026-10-25#Refinements 5]]
- **Fewer popups:** no "ingot collected", no "Ingot loaded", no pause on the finished blade; a new metal is a small toast, not a card (Allen: "too intrusive"). [[Sessions/2026-10-25#Refinements 5]]
- **Shaping prototype: the guide lines on the blade, and sanding by hand** (Allen: "the smoothing should be another manual step ... hold mouse down and basically 'sand down' the bumps"; picked "Overlay on the blade" and "Stops at the line"). The diagram is gone; supersedes the one-click Smooth of Refinement 4. [[Sessions/2026-10-25#Refinements 5]]
- **Shaping prototype: the diagram is the guide again, at the top; no lines on the blade** (Allen: "is there no way to show it graphically instead of having physical lines? if not, lets bring back the original shape/guideline gui above"). Lines on the 3D blade (thin, glowing, ledges, screen-projected) can't show millimetres at the full-blade camera (~13 px a cm); the magnified diagram can. The blade stays the main view, centred below the panel; the red/blue tint on the steel stays. Supersedes "Overlay on the blade" earlier the same day. [[Sessions/2026-10-25#Refinements 5]]
- **Shaping prototype: the guide is a true-shape silhouette** (Allen: "is the shape in the preview representative of the actual blade in game? it doesnt seem that way", then "true-shape silhouette: curved, with the point, width stretched evenly about 2.5x, my blade filled and the target as an outline"). It replaces the straightened width chart (widths under 1.4 cm cut off). Precision falls from ~47 to ~28 px per cm of width, for a picture that looks like the blade. [[Sessions/2026-10-25#Refinements 5]]
- **A Studio-only Test Anvil runs the new shaping process start to finish** (Allen: "take the prototype and apply it to a sample anvil so i can test the flow start to finish"; picked a new Studio-only anvil, match % = blade quality, "every sword"). Anvils 1 and 2 keep the current forging. The rules scale to each blade's width; double-edged blades are shaped on both edges. The client's match is trusted only because it's Studio-only. [[Sessions/2026-10-25#Refinements 5]]
- **FreeStylized swap: Wood Planks 04 shop floors, Floor Tiles 01 workshop floor, Wood Planks 23 crates** (Allen: "replace some of our current textures with appropriate textures", suggesting Wood Planks 23 and Floor Tiles 09 or 01). Claude's picks: Wood Planks 23 has a cloth banner in every tile, so it dresses the crates, not floors; Floor Tiles 01 over 09 (cleaner). [[Sessions/2026-10-25#FreeStylized texture swap: shop floor, workshop floor, crates]]
- **The delivery board is FreeStylized's Notice Board 01; shelves and tables wear Wood 09** (Allen: "Can we add this notice board ... to replace the delivery board", "the wood material on the shelves and tables, can we find a replacement on the site too"). Claude's defaults: same spot and height as the old board, the Storage-box icon and paper slips dropped (the board's own papers show what it is); Wood 09 on every plain-Wood part of the shop displays, cashier counter and workshop stations, colours kept. [[Sessions/2026-10-25#Notice board for deliveries, stylized wood on shelves and tables]]
- **Display tables: no brass corner caps, a plain plastic top** (Allen: "remove the corner caps from the tables. Make the table top have a plastic finish, no wood grains"). SmoothPlastic, colour kept; the legs, aprons and shelf keep Wood 09. Then the sword stands on them too (Allen: "make the sword stands on the table also plastic"); their small gold caps stay metal. [[Sessions/2026-10-25#Display tables: no corner caps, plastic top]]

## Etch refinement — 2026-10-25

- **Etch motifs are sized from the blade width capped at 7 cm, centred on a wider face, and stop where the edge starts rising into a sloped tip** (Allen: "Refine the etchings. the falling stars look off. and fix the issues you pointed out", with a pasted critique: "Fix zanbatō placement, scale, and tip distortion"). Claude's defaults: 7 cm, a slope of 0.25 cm per cm past 40% of the blade, half a width short of it. Flame follows the same cap, so its waves return on the zanbatō. [[Sessions/2026-10-25#Etch refinement]]
- **The etch halo scales with the blade** (critique: the fixed halo "occupies a much larger fraction of a tantō's width than a zanbatō's"). Claude's default: 7% of the capped mean width. Fracture's shards get a crisp border half a fault wide instead of the soft halo. [[Sessions/2026-10-25#Etch refinement]]
- **Eclipse's mouths and Woven Wind's crossings are kept open; Stormroot starts from a narrow root with thinner branches; Fracture is one connected band; Falling Stars are bigger stars with thin trails streaming back toward the guard** (the critique's list). Claude's numbers are in the session note. [[Sessions/2026-10-25#Etch refinement]]
- **Flame's band is the metal colour lifted toward white, like the stroked marks** (critique: "Check contrast on Iron, Black Iron, Gold, and a busy textured material at actual phone size"; Iron's band vanished on Iron). The Etching Table's show already drew it that way. [[Sessions/2026-10-25#Etch refinement]]
- **Shaping prototype: hold to hammer; stronger, wider strikes; 2× accuracy** (Allen: "it takes too many manual hits to fully forge the shape", "maybe instead of manually hitting they can also hold down to hit automatically?", "accuracy needs to be a bit more generous"). Holding keeps striking at the animation's beat, wherever you aim. `strength` 0.4 cm, `radius` 11% (was 0.15, 7%); `perfect` 0.16 cm, `tolerance` 0.5 cm (was 0.08, 0.25). No fixed strike count: "it just depends on the shape it starts at compared to the silhouette". Still an experiment. [[Sessions/2026-10-25#Anvil shaping: fewer hits, a true-width guide, sanding any time]]
- **Shaping prototype: the silhouette is drawn at true width across the screen** (Allen: "the guide is not representative of the blade itself"). Replaces the 2.5× width stretch decided earlier the same day. [[Sessions/2026-10-25#Anvil shaping: fewer hits, a true-width guide, sanding any time]]
- **Shaping prototype: sand any time; sanding rounds bumps off first, never past the line** (Allen: "players should be able to smooth whenever", "a sharp rough bump would turn smoother"). Replaces the 90% gate. [[Sessions/2026-10-25#Anvil shaping: fewer hits, a true-width guide, sanding any time]]
- **Test Anvil blades keep the shape you hammered** (Allen: "wire in our test anvil so that i can also hilt the blade produced from it", then "Keep the shape I made"). The blade stores its widths, and the Hilt Bench, held and displayed swords, cards, etching and polishing all draw them; polishing a shaped blade changes only its finish. Studio only, like the Test Anvil. [[Sessions/2026-10-26#Test Anvil blades keep their shape]]
- **Sword meshes built on a client are baked to static content and their live meshes freed** (Allen picked "convert each finished blade into a regular MeshPart, then free the live mesh", then "Yes, build the bake into SwordMeshCache"). Uses `AssetService:CreateDataModelContentAsync`, which Roblox keeps out of the EditableMesh/EditableImage budgets. Each client bakes what it shows; baking once on the server is left for later. [[Sessions/2026-10-26#Live sword meshes are baked]]
- **Hotbar slots are 84 × 94 with 2 px edges and a whole-slot halftone** (Allen: "the toolbars borders are too thick. the half-tones are only on a part of the entire box ... i want to be able to fit 6 in the toolbar including the backpack, so make them a bit smaller"). Claude's numbers: 0.84 scale, the "diagonal" halftone. New stacks fill the bar up to 9 regardless of the screen. [[Sessions/2026-10-26#Smaller hotbar]]
- **Ingots are named by their metal alone ("Iron", "Hot Iron")** (Allen: "we don't need to say "ingot" if its like "iron ingot" for example, its obvious its an ingot already"). Tool names, the hotbar and the rack picker. [[Sessions/2026-10-26#Ingot names without "Ingot"]]

- **Station pickers show 2 rows × 3 columns and scroll down** (Allen: "ideally the items scroll vertically and we can have a 2x3 visible"). Replaces 2 × 2 on desktop and one sideways row on phones. [[Sessions/2026-10-26#Fixes: picker grid, mail rework, mailbox]]
- **No notice when a hot ingot comes out of the furnace** (Allen: "remove that"). [[Sessions/2026-10-26#Fixes: picker grid, mail rework, mailbox]]
- **A bought Mailbox gates the mail** (Allen: "a physical mailbox players buy at the start during the tutorial which flows nicely into this"). 15 coins, a pad right of the shop door, prompted after the first sale (Allen picked "After 1st sale, ~15 coins"). The Mail button and every letter or order wait for it. [[Sessions/2026-10-26#Fixes: picker grid, mail rework, mailbox]]
- **Letters come from a named cast with faces** (Allen: "see the image of the NPC that sent it"; picked "3D NPC headshot"). Ten villagers in `Shared/Senders`; the Guard Captain is Takeda, the Wandering Swordsman Musashi. [[Sessions/2026-10-26#Fixes: picker grid, mail rework, mailbox]]
- **New letters pop up middle right; tapping opens the letter** (Allen picked "Middle right"). [[Sessions/2026-10-26#Fixes: picker grid, mail rework, mailbox]]
- **The mail is a list of letters with a letter view, Accept / Decline, Send on the row, history at the bottom and an Active only toggle** (Allen's Fixes note). A declined order moves to the history (Allen picked "Move to history"); story letters can't be declined so the tutorial can't stall (Claude's call). Replaces Hand in and "opening the mail reads the letters". [[Sessions/2026-10-26#Fixes: picker grid, mail rework, mailbox]]

- **A shop holder's take-back (bag) markers stand in a straight line just outside the holder's left end, fixed in the world** (later the same day, Allen: "position them all the same based off the sword holder or shelf instead of the sword length"; supersedes "past its pommel"); the + stays in the middle; both circles are 25% smaller (33 px)** (Allen: "the bag icon is to the right so player can see the sword on sale", then "like 25% smaller. also move the backpack to the left ... as i move my camera around the icons get misplaced"). Supersedes the same day's camera-right offset. [[Sessions/2026-10-26#Take-back marker beside the sword]]
- **UI icons come from the Free Icon Pack by @gvesster, 1st Outline versions, unedited** (Allen: "swap out our existing icons with the corresponding ones from the pack. Use the 1st outline versions"). Replaces VectorIcons. Claude's picks where the pack had several: Purple Diamond, Red Gift, Red Shopping Cart, Lighting, Red / Yellow House. [[Sessions/2026-10-26#Free Icon Pack icons]]
- **The Mail button uses Allen's own orange mailbox** (his art, dark border). [[Sessions/2026-10-26#Mailbox icon]]
- **The left HUD column sizes itself with Scale + constraints in StarterGui, no runtime script** (Allen: "Icons are a bit small right now on desktop", then "convert the left column to Scale + constraints in StarterGui and remove the runtime Grow"). Phones keep the old pixel sizes; lanes cap at 445 px, x1.34 on desktop. Claude's numbers: 0.6 lane aspect, 445 px cap. Replaces the same day's runtime `Grow`. [[Sessions/2026-10-26#Bigger left HUD on desktop]]
- **The store upgrades button is the pack's Stats, the collection book its Blue Book** (Allen: "use Stats 1st Outline instead of the house icon for shop upgrades. Use a book instead of the sword icon"). Blue is Claude's pick. [[Sessions/2026-10-26#Stats and book icons, letters marked read]]
- **Letters are marked read when opened (saved); the Mail dot and the New chip mean unread letters only** (Allen: "Once a letter has been read, it should be marked as read. so the mailbox notification be can settled once all messages are read"). The dot no longer lights for a sword you could send. [[Sessions/2026-10-26#Stats and book icons, letters marked read]]
- **Staff tabs open a roster of their members first, like Crafters; the Staff page has a header Back** (Allen: "make it so its like Crafters, where you can select your cashier or shop promoter. so we can add in the future more cashiers or shop promoters"). All four staff roles, Claude's call. Still one per role in the save. [[Sessions/2026-10-26#Staff rosters in the crew window]]
- **Plots are 84 × 122 with a low wooden fence round them** (Allen: "Make the plot each player owns larger, and add fences around it so it's clear where a player's plot starts and ends"; picked option B and "Low wooden fence, gaps" from a top-down capture). The fence takes in the delivery bay, a side yard and a back yard; it's open at the shop door, the bay mouth and a 6-stud gap into the side yard. Lanes between lots shrink to 12.5 studs; the curb edges are gone. [[Sessions/2026-10-26#Bigger plots with a fence]]
- **Picker cards get a warm light pool behind the item, a dark rim round it, brighter blades, and no chip when every card shares it** (Allen: "our picker ui looks very dull … no need to have the "store" repeated on all of the buttons", then picked mockup D). Coin prices always show. Then: square charcoal-to-black cards with a thin (1.5 px) rim (gold, then white: "use white"), a bigger sword view with the name below and chips as a corner tag, no light pool; picker windows show a tiled seigaiha pattern under half-clear brown ("Apply seigaiha C at 0.5") and cast a drop shadow; shop take-back bags sit on the table or shelf beside the hilt, a big sword's in its middle; picker and station GUIs have square corners except true circles (Allen: "make all the station guis and picker guis have 0 corner radius") (Allen picked "Charcoal + gold rim" and the thinnest rim from comparison boards). [[Sessions/2026-10-26#Brighter picker cards]]
- **Chunky low-poly style, piloted on the Ore Roller lever** (Allen's reference: the clay game's Clay Reroll lever, "chunky, small bevels on every edge, flat colours"). The style uses simple convex shapes with big design chamfers, a one-segment 0.04 chamfer on every edge, flat shading and one flat colour per part. The lever was rebuilt to match the reference's shape (loaf body, orange side panel, sage chamfered base) with the same swing. The knob is a plain Roblox Ball part (Allen: "just use a regular roblox sphere part instead of the sphere you made in blender"). Claude's defaults: the reference's colours darkened for the warm workshop light; footprint 2.6 × 3.5; the rest of the level waits for Allen's OK on the look. [[Sessions/2026-10-26#Chunky low-poly style: Ore Roller lever pilot]]
- **The ore stands stand in two straight rows** (Allen: "make the stands align, right now the back stands are not aligned with front one"): each back stand directly behind a front one, replacing the half-gap stagger. **The lever is built from Roblox Parts** (Allen: "use the parts one"), not the Blender meshes; the chunky style can be done in Parts with Wedges and CornerWedges for the big chamfers, without the small edge chamfers. [[Sessions/2026-10-26#Ore stands aligned, parts lever in]]
- **World text looks like the clay game's labels and is built in Studio** (Allen: "apply these font changes to all of our in game font ... the physical in game ones like the upgrade buttons, npc names", "Ideally I want most of the texts built as assets in studio"). Gotham Bold with a thick, rounded near-black outline that scales with the text; ore labels show the rarity on its own smaller line. Every billboard is a template in `ReplicatedStorage.WorldText` that scripts clone and fill. Claude's defaults: painted kanji signs, plated signs, the speech bubble and the Buy button keep their look; outline 0.2 of the text height. [[Sessions/2026-10-26#World text: clay-game labels, built as Studio templates]]

## Tips — 2026-10-26

- **Alt frees the mouse in first person** (Allen, tips.md: "add an option for the user to hold ALT while in first POV to move their mouse to select things"). Claude's defaults: either Alt key, also in shift lock, the camera holds still while it's held, no on-screen hint. [[Sessions/2026-10-26#Tips document (tips.md)]]
- **The tutorial spotlight gets an arrow** (Allen: "add an arrow pointing at highlighted items"): the world arrow's chevrons, bobbing over the spotlit button (under it near the top of the screen). Adds to Refinements 5's no-ring spotlight; there is still no ring.
- **The workshop door is 8.8 studs wide** (Allen: "make the door to the workshop a bit bigger. (not the store entrance)"), was 5.8; same height. Claude's default: 1.5 wider on each side.
- **With two or more swords that fit a letter, you choose which to send** (Allen: "allow the user to select which one will be sent"). One fitting sword still sends at once (Allen confirmed). Supersedes Refinements 5's "Send gives the cheapest that fits, no picker" for the 2+ case.
- **Pad steps say how to buy** (Allen: "make it clear how to buy upgrades"): "Stand on the pad to buy …", then "Press Buy" on the pad.
- **Carried blades are inventory items** (Allen: "after finishing the blades on the anvil, they don't show up in the inventory ... make sure they are visible"): each blade is a hotbar item, a bare blade you can hold, until its hilt goes on.

## Tutorial fixes — 2026-10-27

- **Saving up for a blueprint, the guide names your best blade** (Allen: "the guide should name the best blueprint I know while I save up"): "Forge a Wakizashi" while you save for the Katana Sword, not "Forge a Tantō". Big swords are never named (only their stand takes one). Found in [[Archive/Design/Progression and Economy Review — 2026-10-27#10. The tutorial]]. [[Sessions/2026-10-27#Tutorial fixes: the best blade, a Gem Shop step that ends on a buy]]
- **The Gem Shop step ends on a buy** (Allen: "the Gem Shop step should end on something I can buy. Use your defaults."). Claude's defaults: Better Advertising's first level costs **5 gems** (was 25), the day-1 daily reward; the step comes once your gems cover it, spotlights its Buy button with "Buy a shop boost with your gems", and buying ends it. Supersedes Refinements 5's "Gems buy boosts for your shop that last" over the whole shop.

## Economy pass 2 direction — 2026-10-27

- **Spins stay free and unlimited; the economy is priced for players who pick rare ore, with a scrap bin for leftover ingots** (Allen: "Keep unlimited free spins. Plan the economy pass around players picking rare ore, plus a scrap bin for leftover ingots."). Answers the review's ore question ([[Archive/Design/Progression and Economy Review — 2026-10-27#Questions for Allen]]); keeps Refinements 3's "spin as many times as they want" and 2026-10-24's "remove this limit". Plan: [[Design/Economy Pass 2]]. The bin was dropped in the walkthrough below.
- **The next sword and the next employee take a meaningful amount of time** (Allen: "plan the economy based around the upgrades, it should take a meaningful amount of time to get the next sword and to also get an employee"). He walked through the plan the same day (below); building it waits for his go-ahead. [[Sessions/2026-10-27#Economy pass 2 plan]]

Allen's answers, walking through [[Design/Economy Pass 2]] ("Walk through pass 2 with me and ask me about each"). [[Sessions/2026-10-27#Economy pass 2 walkthrough]]

- **Prices are built for a Rare+ picker** (Allen picked it over an Epic+ hunter).
- **The goal order stays:** Katana, Longsword, Sword Crafter, Falchion, Stocker, Shop Promoter, Gladius, Cashier, Sword Etcher, then the Zanbatō and the crew.
- **8 h to today's last floor pad for a casual picker** (Allen picked "8 h" over 5 h 30 and 12 h); the Katana stays at 20 min and everything after it stretches. Answers the review's length question.
- **A goal is the last 40% of its stage;** the small buys share the rest (Allen kept 40% over 60% and 25%).
- **Each stage's pads and cart levels wait for the previous goal** (Allen: "Pads and cart levels").
- **The Sword Crafter comes trained on your best sword** (Allen: "Trained on hire").
- **The tutorial keeps its prices:** Mailbox 15, Wakizashi Sword 60, Ore Slot 2 75 (Allen: "Keep them").
- **The Upgraded Store stays in the Falchion stage** (Allen picked it over the Longsword stage).
- **A pull replaces the ore left on the lid; there is no scrap bin and scrapping pays nothing** (Allen: "just spin new ones, existing ones are replaced", "Nothing", "no scrap bin"). Supersedes 2026-10-23's "a pull first puts the ore still waiting into your hands" and the plan's bin.
- **The bag gets a trash icon:** drag a stack onto it to throw the whole stack away (Allen: "opening inventory, there should be a trash icon that allows you to trash it", then "Drag a stack onto it"). Ingots, blades and swords can be trashed (Allen: "Ingots and swords"), and it never asks first (Allen: "Never").
- **The tutorial teaches the pick:** its first roll asks you to pull again before you collect (Allen: "Inside the tutorial"). Claude's default: the first pull always brings a Common and the second an Uncommon.
- **Up to three sword crafters work at once,** one each at Anvils 2–4 beside your own anvil; any crafter you've bought can replace a working one (Allen: "Should be at most 3 active sword crafter at a time. different crafters bought can replace active ones if players want. That means 3 anvils can exist"). Supersedes Refinements 2's "One at Anvil 2".
- **The Zanbatō is sold,** a late goal after the Gladius with the Big Sword Stand in its stage (Allen: "Sell the Zanbatō"). Answers the review's Big Sword Stand question.
- **The Upgraded Store's upper storey is cream plaster, not black kura** (Allen: "Repaint the Upgraded Store's upper storey and gables to the same cream plaster as the walls"). Window trim, shutters and the hachimaki band stay dark. [[Sessions/2026-10-27#Upgraded Store upper storey made cream]]

Economy pass 2, built the same day (Allen: "Build Economy Pass 2 as decided"), with Claude's defaults where the plan left a choice. [[Sessions/2026-10-27#Economy pass 2 built]]

- **Anvil 4 comes after Anvil 3, not after Haru:** pads can only wait for upgrades, and crew hires aren't upgrades.
- **Anvils 3 and 4 stand on the workshop floor either side of the hearth,** copies of Anvil 2 turned the same way: Anvil 3 right of it toward the furnaces, Anvil 4 between it and the Hilt Bench; each pad on its anvil's spot. They're your anvils too, like Anvil 2.
- **A new hire works at a free bought crafter anvil;** with none free they wait in the crew window until you put them somewhere.
- **The bag's trash is a copy of the bag button with the pack's Trash Can, over the open bag's top-right corner;** dropping a stack there throws the whole stack away.
- **A save from before keeps its crafters but not where they work** (no live players, so no carry-over): put them back on the crew window's Station page, or Reset progress.

Customer rate, the same day ([[Design/Economy Pass 2#9. Customer rate]]): walk-ins outnumbered swords 5–11× all game, so no customer upgrade or event mattered.

- **A walk-in every 45–75 s, Better Advertising 36–64, 30–52 and 22–42 s; reputation no longer shortens the gap; the tutorial keeps 12–22 s until Ore Slot 2** (Allen: "Apply the recommended customer rate"). Supersedes 2026-10-22's 12–22 s base and reputation's 6% a rank. [[Sessions/2026-10-27#Customer rate applied]]

## Monetization direction — 2026-10-27

Allen: "research all the potential developer products we can have for purchase with robux. gamepasses, 1 starter pack. gamepasses that ease the experience, or provide better bonuses, luck, etc. 1 of them in my mind is an auto ore roller that shows up on your screen for purchase whenever youre near the ore roller itself. its would be a small popup with a lock icon to show you can buy it." Plan: [[Design/Monetization Plan]]. Nothing built.

- **An Auto Ore Roller game pass, offered by a small on-screen popup with a lock icon whenever you're near your ore roller** (Allen's design). Claude's defaults: the pack's Auto Open icon with the Lock on its corner, no text; after purchase the same button is the on/off switch; it stops while a kept rarity (Rare+ to start) lies on a stand.
- **Exactly one Starter Pack** (Allen). Claude's proposal: a 99 R$ game pass with 100 gems, a Gold ingot and a buyers-only etch; offered by the Shop button's dot, no popup.
- **GDD §15's rule stands:** Robux speeds things up or adds style; everything can be earned.
- **Waiting on Allen:** paid luck or not (merging is the only luck since 2026-10-25), the Starter Pack's contents, 2× Coins and coin crates, roll-only or auto-collect, and the restricted-region alternative for luck. [[Sessions/2026-10-27#Monetization plan]]

## Monetization decisions — 2026-10-27

Allen walked through [[Design/Monetization Plan]]'s five questions in two rounds; this settles the open points of "Monetization direction" above. Nothing built.

- **Luck: a Lucky Ore pass, luck one merge higher (+0.5)** (Allen: "It should be an increased luck pass, but doesn't have to be necessarily 2x. I notice in the clay game they have the odds available"). Its odds panel copies Create Pottery!'s Lucky Clay panel: Now → With Lucky Ore, before Roblox's purchase window.
- **Restricted regions:** players where paid random items are restricted can buy an ingot of a found metal (Epic or Legendary, already in their book) instead of Lucky Ore and bought rain.
- **Starter Pack: 100 gems and a Gold ingot, 99 R$, no etch** (Allen: "keep the gems, the ingots, no buyers-only etch"). Claude's default: the Etch Pack is dropped too.
- **Coins and more** (Allen: "50% more coins, coin packs, gem packs, control the weather ( control the temporary server boost), vip pass gives +10% on every sale"): 50% More Coins replaces 2× Coins; VIP gives +10% on every sale; coin packs and gem packs are in. Claude's defaults: the 30-min 2× Coins, Customer Rush and Lucky Hour are dropped; Swift Boots stays.
- **Four weathers, also bought** (Allen: "the weather should only be around 5 minutes. sunshine can give 2x more customers, rainbow can give higher chance of vip customer, rain gives higher luck for rolling, snowing for more mail/custom requests"), with Robux or gems (Allen picked "The weather too" for gems). Replaces 2026-10-19's "other weather stays at the normal baseline" and "no paid weather controls". Claude's defaults: rain +0.5 luck, snow mail twice as often, 79 R$ or 60 gems, the buyer named.
- **Auto Ore Roller:** rolls only, with the keep chip starting at Rare+.

[[Sessions/2026-10-27#Monetization walkthrough]]

## Store items on Roblox — 2026-10-27

Allen: "I'll give the API key game-pass and developer-product permission; create the passes and products through Open Cloud." Claude created the plan's 6 passes and 11 developer products in universe 10771581195 ([[Design/Monetization Plan#11. On Roblox (created 2026-10-27)]]). Claude's defaults: everything off sale until its feature is built; no images; managed pricing left off (the API's default); Lucky Ore's and Rain's descriptions carry the odds. [[Sessions/2026-10-27#Passes and products created]]

## Pass features — 2026-10-27

Allen: "after this, work on the actual features the passes give". Built, not played ([[Design/Monetization Plan#12. Built (2026-10-27, not played)]]). Claude's defaults:

- VIP's "gold name tag" is a gold [VIP] before the name in chat (TextChatService).
- The coin passes multiply (×1.65 with both) and pay when the coins land; the Cashier's till holds boosted coins.
- Lucky Ore adds +0.5 to the merges' luck.
- The Auto Ore Roller starts off; its switch and keep chip are saved through the Settings' `Setting` remote; its button shows within 12 studs of the lever.
- In Studio, pass ownership comes from StoreService's attributes, all off.

[[Sessions/2026-10-27#Pass features built]]

## Shop tension pass — 2026-10-27

Allen picked all three suggestions from [[Design/Pottery Playthrough 2026-10-27]], then "Recommended" for every pick ([[Design/Shop Tension Pass]]):
- **VIP buyers:** 5% of walk-ins after the tutorial, ×1.75–2.25 the value, no raise, 60 s to answer. Upward only, keeping "no lowball offers".
- **No browsers who leave without an offer.** Customers say a line about the sword instead.
- **Sunshine weather, free:** walk-ins ×2 for 5 minutes, every 15–25 minutes, server-wide, not in the tutorial. The bought weathers and the other three come later.
- **Faster Heating 10 / 6 / 4 / 3 s.**
- **The 45 s offer timer stays** until a playtest counts the expired offers (more than 1 in 4 → 60 s).
- Claude's defaults: no head tag on a VIP (a gold outfit and a gold card instead, so it doesn't cover the speech bubble); the Sunshine chip is text, because the icon pack has no sun.

[[Sessions/2026-10-27#Shop tension pass built]]

## Shop page — 2026-10-27

Allen: "Build the Shop page next: the other passes with live prices, the Starter Pack's dot and Lucky Ore's odds panel." Built, not played ([[Design/Monetization Plan#12. Built (2026-10-27, not played)]]). Claude's defaults:

- All six passes as a scrolling list of cards like the Gem Shop's (the Auto Ore Roller too), with the pack's icons and its Golden Robux on the prices; "—" on a grey button while a pass is off sale.
- The Starter Pack's dot shows after the tutorial until the Shop is opened, once per session, not until it's bought.
- Lucky Ore's Buy always opens its odds panel first and the purchase is made from there; where paid random items are restricted, its card is hidden until the found-metal ingot (a developer product) exists.

[[Sessions/2026-10-27#Shop page built]]

## Passes on sale — 2026-10-27

Allen: "Put all six passes on sale while the game is private, so the Shop shows prices and I can test buying." All six went on sale through Open Cloud, Lucky Ore included; the developer products stay off sale until built. Before the game goes public, Lucky Ore's Store tab question must be settled ([[Design/Monetization Plan#Open]]). [[Sessions/2026-10-27#Passes on sale]]

## Message bars, floor arrows, red closes, paper letters — 2026-10-27

Allen: tutorial and popup messages on a dark vignette bar; green floor arrows plus a Highlight instead of the bouncing arrow; a red square X on every window, square corners and drop shadows; letters that look like letters. All applied ([[Sessions/2026-10-27#Message bars, floor arrows, red closes, paper letters]]). Supersedes the GuideArrow chevrons, the rounded window corners and the dark round close rings.

## Mailbox from the start, tutorial ends at the first sale — 2026-10-28

Allen (Fixes list): "instead of having player buy the mailbox, just have the mailbox already there. the tutorial finishes after the player makes a sale. then after a few minutes they receive mail asking for a sword." Every plot has the Mailbox (no pad); story letters come 45 s after the sale that makes them due (Allen: "they should get it in about 45 seconds"; 150 s at first). Also from that list: furnace timers through walls on your own plot, the anvil's shape panel replaced by a progress bar, message bars sized to their text, the mail popup middle right. [[Sessions/2026-10-28#Fixes 2026-10-28]]. Supersedes the 15-coin Mailbox pad (2026-10-26).

## The first letter asks for a metal — 2026-10-28

Allen: "the first tutorial quest should not specifically be a wakizashi sword ... the quest should be able to be completed at the progress they're at. the quest should specify the type of material, probably a common material and players should be prompted to keep spinning until they find it. once the metal is found, there should be some message that says that the material is used in the quest." Takeda's letter now wants an Iron Tantō, any quality (round 3, Allen: "it should specifically ask for a sword shape"; the anvil's blade picker spotlights the shape); pulls lean to Iron (1 in 3) while it's open; the ore shows "Takeda wants this!". Also: straight-line guide arrows, a smaller letter popup. [[Sessions/2026-10-28#Fixes 2026-10-28, round 2]]. Supersedes the Wakizashi of any metal at 90%+ (2026-10-22).

## Phone text floor — 2026-10-28

Allen: "the gui on mobile is too small", then "Yes, do all windows". Window text reads at least 14 px on an 844 × 390 phone after the window's fit scale (20 px in a 0.7 window). [[Sessions/2026-10-28#Phone text pass]].

## Station prompts on screen — 2026-10-28

Allen: "instead of having the 'E to do x' gui in the game ... the button is actualy on their screen and they hold that instead" (a reference with an "E Block / Pick Up" card). Station prompts show as one on-screen card over the hotbar that you tap or hold; the shop holders and racks keep their markers. Built in Studio as an asset (Allen: "not during runtime"). [[Sessions/2026-10-28#On-screen action button]].

## Ghost clicks instead of the bouncing arrow — 2026-10-28

Allen: "the red and green bouncing arrow is redundant now if we have the spotlight effect"; "we should have a ghost click animation/tween and spotlight effect when opening the pickers to show exactly what to click". The spotlight's bouncing arrow is gone; in guided steps the pickers spotlight the card to pick and a ghost cursor taps it (only there: "We dont need the ghost for everything"). Supersedes the spotlight arrow (Tips 2026-10-26). [[Sessions/2026-10-28#Playtest fixes: arrows, forge, cards, ghost clicks]].

## Roblox's prompts back; the on-screen card is switched off — 2026-10-28

Allen: "lets revert the change to have the default roblox click instead of our custom button. make it so we can toggle it on or off if i change my mind". Station prompts use Roblox's own look; the card stays built and comes back by ticking `StarterGui.ActionGui.Enabled`. Supersedes "Station prompts on screen" (same day) while the switch is off. [[Sessions/2026-10-28#Roblox's prompts back, the card behind a switch]].

## Fixes 2026-10-28, more — 2026-10-28

No popup for a newly made sword; bigger phone picker cards with one row showing (Allen: "Bigger cards always"); the mail closes after a Send and the coins fly in; a letter preview on each mail row; Takeda's first letter pays 5 gems (enough for Better Advertising) and the Gem Shop step waits for that; Gem Shop stats as multipliers (Customers 1x → 1.2x). [[Sessions/2026-10-28#Six more: popups, phone cards, Send coins, previews, gems, stats]].

## Cascading ore roll — 2026-10-28

Allen: "a cascading loot roller/generator", then "Yes, build it". The roll still draws one metal with the same odds; the box's show starts every ore as Common and climbs it a tier at a time (pop, flash, rising ding) to that result, all slots at once, with no near-miss show. The show lasts as long as the best ore needs (1.75 s for a Common to 4.5 s for a Secret; was 3.6 s). Also: lever hold 1 s (was 1.5). Supersedes the flicker-through-every-metal show (2026-10-22/24). [[Sessions/2026-10-28#Cascading ore roll]].

## Ore Slots: always the next one — 2026-10-28

Allen: "player should always be able to buy the next ore upgrade so once they have 6, they can merge and keep re-doing that cycle". Ore Slots 4–6 need only the slot before; prices (75 / 520 / 3,900 / 26,000 / 100,000, ×1.5 per merge) pace them. Supersedes Economy pass 2's Katana / Falchion / Cashier gates on Ore Slots 4–6 (2026-10-27). [[Sessions/2026-10-28#Ore Slots ungated]].

## NEW means first rolled — 2026-10-28

Allen: "it would be no longer new if we've rolled it already". The lid's NEW shows only on a metal's first roll (saved), on its own line above the name. The collection book still counts collected metals. Supersedes NEW-until-collected (2026-10-22). [[Sessions/2026-10-28#NEW on the first roll, on its own line]].

## Auto-Roll pill, no New metal toast — 2026-10-28

Allen: "it should be in the middle of the screen and say Auto-Roll. smaller icon as well"; "Remove the 'new metal' popup". Auto-Roll is a pill over the hotbar with a small icon and its name; the first collect of a metal shows no message (the lid's NEW does it). Supersedes the square Auto Ore Roller button on the right (2026-10-27) and the New metal toast (Refinements 5). [[Sessions/2026-10-28#No New metal toast; the Auto-Roll pill]].


## Chunky button style — 2026-10-28

Allen, of another game's shop: "a gradient in the middle and thick borders". Trying it on the upgrade Buy button first: near-black 4 px outline, a darker inner rim, a vertical gradient lighter through the middle, a drop shadow. [[Sessions/2026-10-28#Chunky Buy button]].
