Allen: "maybe it stops on ores that a player needs to complete a quest/request. or it can stop on rare+, uncommon+ or whatever the player wants to configure"; "towards the top of the screen. a little under the tutorial messages". Two saved chips: a rarity (Uncommon+ to Legendary+, or off; default Rare+) and Quests (stop on an ore an open letter or rarer-metal order needs; default on). The pill sits top centre under the guide's line. Supersedes the keep chip's Rare/Epic/Legendary/Any (2026-10-27) and the bottom-centre pill (earlier today). [[Sessions/2026-10-28#Auto-Roll stop settings, at the top]].

A fixed bar "Auto-Roll" with an On/Off switch and a gear; the gear opens an Auto-Roll window like Settings (Stop at rarity, Quest ores). Supersedes the chips under the pill (same day). [[Sessions/2026-10-28#Auto-Roll bar and its window]].

Allen: "make the rest of our picker UIs and shop uis to match the border radius and the thickness"; "use black border". Windows 16 px corners / 5 px outline, cards and rows 12 / 4, buttons 10 / 4, chips 8 (design pixels under each window's `Fit` scale), outlines near-black 16,20,16. Supersedes square corners and thin brown edges on these windows. [[Sessions/2026-10-28#Picker and shop UIs match the Buy button]].

## Rounded, thick black edges on every menu — 2026-10-28

Allen: "make the rest of our picker UIs and shop uis to match the border radius and the thickness"; "use black border"; "apply this to the other menus too". Windows 16 px corners / 5 px outline, cards 12 / 4, buttons 10 / 4, chips 8, outlines near-black 16,20,16 (state colours kept). Supersedes square corners and thin brown edges. [[Sessions/2026-10-28#Menus match the Buy button: rounded, thick black edges]].

Allen, of another game's shop: "have our shop text sit between the border, so there's not as much wasted space on the top". Windows whose header is only a title and X put the title on the top-left border and the X over the corner. [[Sessions/2026-10-28#Titles on the border; the Shop fits an iPhone 17]].

## Ingot book sections by rarity — 2026-10-28

Allen: "separating them by rarities and with a divider". The book shows a section per rarity, a coloured divider over each. [[Sessions/2026-10-28#Ingot book grouped by rarity]].

Allen: "maybe our current one is good, but a bit brighter?" Kept the workshop brown over three vivid palettes (ember orange, royal purple, sky blue), about 1.9× brighter: windows 120,72,40 → 92,54,30, cards 158,98,58 → 128,78,44. [[Sessions/2026-10-28#Brighter browns; the Gem Shop is Upgrades]].

## The Gem Shop is called Upgrades — 2026-10-28

Allen: "instead of gem shop, since its really a store upgrades shop, it should be called Upgrades". Player-facing name only. [[Sessions/2026-10-28#Brighter browns; the Gem Shop is Upgrades]].

## Shop: a featured Starter Pack over a grid — 2026-10-28

Allen: "like a grid and also highlighting the starter pack or some good deal as larger cards, and then as they scroll there's smaller cards". The Starter Pack is a big gold card first until bought; the other passes are a two-wide grid of smaller cards. Supersedes the one-row-per-pass list. [[Sessions/2026-10-28#Shop as a featured card and a grid]].

## Shop: GAME PASSES and CURRENCY, a colour per card — 2026-10-28

Allen: "our shop should be separated into game passes and currency purchases", "the background to also be a bit different and bright for each purchase with a large icon", "our shop on desktop could be bigger". Two sections; each card its own bright colour with a big icon on rays; the Shop scales up to 1.5 on big screens; the Starter Pack is hidden once owned. Currency packs show but don't sell until a ProcessReceipt exists. [[Sessions/2026-10-28#Shop sections, bright cards, bigger on desktop; Auto-Roll up top]].

## Auto-Roll bar in the guide line's spot — 2026-10-28

Allen: "the auto-roll is a bit too low. can we have it where the tutorial message usually is". Top centre, moving under the guide's line while one shows. [[Sessions/2026-10-28#Shop sections, bright cards, bigger on desktop; Auto-Roll up top]].

## Coin packs are minutes of measured income — 2026-10-28

The plan's "30 min or 2 h of your current income, worked out on the server" made concrete: coins from sales over the last 15 minutes (or since joining, at least 5), floor 50 a minute. Receipts are saved before Roblox is told they're granted. [[Sessions/2026-10-28#Gem and coin packs sell: ProcessReceipt; Buy buttons shaded]].

## Short of coins or gems: offer the covering pack — 2026-10-28

Allen: "when buying an upgrade they don't have enough for, we should popup a currency purchase for robux with the small amount needed to complete the purchase"; "Yes, offer a pack". The smallest coin or gem pack that covers the gap pops up over the notice or shake, after the tutorial and while it's on sale. **Supersedes** the Monetization Plan / economy review rule "an unaffordable pad keeps its shake, not a Robux prompt". [[Sessions/2026-10-28#Short of coins or gems: offer the pack that covers it]].

## Bought upgrades build themselves — 2026-10-28

Allen: "when i buy another stand in the shop, the pieces all come together". Every upgrade item assembles on purchase: pieces drop in from above, lowest first, about 1.3 s; client-only, no sound yet. [[Sessions/2026-10-28#Bought upgrades build themselves]].

## Coin packs are fixed amounts; Shop cards saturated — 2026-10-28

Allen: "change the description to a fixed amount, not based on time of income": 5,000 coins (49 R$) and 20,000 coins (149 R$). **Supersedes** "Coin packs are minutes of measured income" (same day). The five packs went on sale while the game is private ("wire in the products"). Shop cards: saturated colours that stay bright, a strong centre glow and rays, a dark window, lime bevel Buy buttons, Fredoka One titles ("ours look boring"). [[Sessions/2026-10-28#Packs on sale with fixed coin amounts; a brighter Shop]].

## Hand-drawn 9-slice edges with a drop shadow — 2026-10-28

Allen, of the reference shops: "could it be they're using an image?", "they also have a box shadow as well". Shop cards, Buy buttons and window use one hand-drawn 9-slice edge image (uneven, heavier at the bottom, soft shadow) instead of `UIStroke`; the other menus still use `UIStroke`. [[Sessions/2026-10-28#Hand-drawn 9-slice edges with a shadow (Shop)]].

## The hand-drawn edge on every menu — 2026-10-28

Allen: "Yes, all menus". Every menu's outlined frames use the one hand-drawn 9-slice edge (shadowed; flush only where a frame clips), centred so auto-sized windows ignore it. Outlines whose colour carries meaning stay plain strokes; scripted highlights (amber, green, heat) still show over the art. Supersedes "Shop only" from earlier the same day. [[Sessions/2026-10-28#The hand-drawn edge on every menu]].

## Only the tutorial's letter is guided — 2026-10-28

Allen: "when i get another message, i get a spotlight onto it again, and it prompts the tutorial steps again". The guide line, arrows and spotlight follow the first letter only; later letters (the Wandering Swordsman's katana and on) are left to the mail button's dot. Supersedes guiding the katana letter "the same way" (2026-10-22). [[Sessions/2026-10-28#Later letters no longer replay the tutorial]].

## Item cards show the metal's skin — 2026-10-28

Allen: "we can see the sword with the skin/material on it when crafting ... i want it back", "same with the ingot selection as well". Blade, sword and ingot cards in a textured or special metal wear its real look; the anvil's shape cards are blades in the chosen ingot's metal, not black silhouettes. Plain metals keep the lighter matte card colour (2026-10-26). [[Sessions/2026-10-28#Blades and ingots on cards wear their metal's skin]].

## Every window's title on its border; outlined text — 2026-10-28

Allen: "make the UIs in the game similar to our shop, where the text is inbetween the border. also the text needs a border". All windows (pickers, Mail, Delivery, the book, Crew too) put the title on the top border and the X on the corner; light menu text has a black outline. The furnace's countdown is white ("set the font color to white"). [[Sessions/2026-10-28#Titles on the border everywhere; outlined text; a white furnace timer]].

## Cards show every metal as in the world — 2026-10-28

Allen: "when using the pickers, they should reflect the ingame sword or ingot textures". Every ingot and blade card wears its metal's in-game look (texture, surface, shine), plain metals included. Supersedes the lighter matte card colour (2026-10-26) for cards that know their metal. [[Sessions/2026-10-28#Every metal's real texture on cards]].

## No overlapping-work tip — 2026-10-28

Allen: "remove it" ("Heat the next ingot first"), "this is not something to teach in tutorial". The guide no longer tells players to heat the next ingot while they work. Supersedes Workshop Urgency phase 1's overlap tip (2026-10-23). [[Sessions/2026-10-28#The "Heat the next ingot first" tip is gone]].

## One button style, one close style, Gotham everywhere — 2026-10-28

Allen: "Use Gotham. Fix 1-3" (after the style audit). Every green action button is the Shop's lime bevel with a glow and hand-drawn edge; every window's close is the red rounded X; card titles are Gotham Heavy (Fredoka dropped); station screens and popups got the menus' brighter browns, rounded corners, hand-drawn edges and outlined text. [[Sessions/2026-10-28#Style audit, then fixes 1-3]].

## No finish popups for polish or etch — 2026-10-28

Allen: "Remove them" (the etch's toast and the polish/etch reward card). Finishing a polish or an etch shows no popup; a partial polish still says what's left. Extends the morning's "no popups after creating a sword". [[Sessions/2026-10-28#Edges in row layouts fixed; no finish popups]].

## Shop-floor pads out of the stage gates — 2026-10-28

Allen, stuck with only the 61,000 Falchion to buy: "Yes, re-gate them", "Both A and B". Wall Racks 3–4 and Display Tables 2–3 no longer wait for a stage goal: each needs only the Upgraded Store or the one before. **Supersedes** their Economy Pass 2 gates (Falchion, Promoter, Stocker); the other stages' gates stay. The economy model's timings for those buys are now optimistic. [[Sessions/2026-10-28#Bigger coin packs; why no more pads show]].

## Upgrade order — 2026-10-28

Allen: "I feel like the upgrades order is not well constructed", then walked through [[Archive/Design/Upgrade Order Review — 2026-10-28]]. Nothing built.

- **The Sword Crafter at about 1 h, before the Longsword; the second crafter at about 3 h and the third at about 6 h** (Allen: "Yes, all three"). The spine becomes Katana, Sword Crafter, Longsword, Haru (Anvil 3), Stocker, Falchion, Mei (Anvil 4), Shop Promoter, Gladius, Cashier, Sword Etcher, Zanbatō, then Kenji and Rin as replacements. **Supersedes** Economy pass 2's "The goal order stays" and its Haru at 9 h 50 and Mei at 11 h 20 (2026-10-27).
- **A new sword teaches the working crafters** (Allen: "New sword teaches crew"). Training stays in the crew window for switching an anvil to another sword; the Falchion (37,000) and Gladius (110,000) training buys go.
- **Two stages open:** a stage's pads appear when the goal before last is bought (Allen: "Two stages open"). Gates otherwise only where the world needs them, so the Falchion no longer waits for the Sword Crafter, the Gladius for the Shop Promoter, the Cashier for the Gladius, the Etcher for the Cashier, the Big Sword Stand for the Etcher or Anvil 3 for the Zanbatō. **Supersedes** "Each stage's pads and cart levels wait for the previous goal" (2026-10-27). Ore Slots and the shop-floor pads stay outside the gates (2026-10-28).
- **The first merge about 2 h in** (Allen: "first merge about 2 hours in"): Ore Slots 5–6 at about 10,000 and 22,000 (now 26,000 and 100,000).
- **Merges keep ×1.5 a re-buy** (Allen: "x1.5, as today").
- **Haru and Mei stay crew-window hires** (Allen: "Crew window, as today").
- Claude's defaults: prices re-fitted with customers in, for a casual picker who buys the cheapest pad on offer, Katana near 20 min and the Zanbatō near 8 h; goal prices only rise along the spine; Haru shows in the crew window once Anvil 3 is bought, Mei once Anvil 4 is, Kenji and Rin after Mei; the crew's gem prices stay; Furnace 2 still waits for Faster Heating III, with the crafter's chain priced as cheap side buys; a goal stays 40% of its stage (the question was dropped: with two stages open and the Ore Slots ungated, the model finds no stretch with two or fewer things on offer).

Modelled (first draft, casual Rare+ picker buying the cheapest pad on offer): Katana 0:23, Sword Crafter 0:56, Longsword 1:41, Haru 2:22, Stocker 3:17, Falchion 3:29, Shop Promoter 4:52, Mei 5:39, Gladius 6:51, Sword Etcher 7:21, Cashier 7:37, Zanbatō 7:52, Kenji 10:51, Rin 13:22; merges at 2:03, 2:51, 4:05, 4:30, 5:07, 6:16 … (11 by 16 h, luck ×6.5); no stretch with two or fewer things on offer before the Zanbatō. [[Sessions/2026-10-28#Upgrade order walkthrough]]

## Fixes2 — 2026-10-28

- **The tutorial ends on sending the first letter** (Allen: "Don't prompt to buy ore slot 2"). Everywhere (Allen: "The end of the letter's quest is end of the tutorial"): the plot's `TutorialDone`, set when the first letter is sent, replaces Ore Slot 2 (or a merge) as the tutorial's end for the Guide, the tutorial walk-in pace, VIPs, weather bonuses and chip, the Auto Ore Roller, the Gem Shop tour and the Starter Pack dot. No "Pull again for a rarer metal" (Allen). ForgeService still makes the first pulls a Common then an Uncommon.
- **Spotlight the whole thing to read** (Allen: "spotlight the entire upgrade, not just the buy button. same with the letter"), and ask to close the mail only once after Accept (Allen: "player should be able to open anything they want without that spotlight again").
- **Finish the anvil early from 9 of 12 strikes** (Allen). The grade is the share of strikes; the value x2 × strikes/12, so 9 strikes = 75% at x1.5 (Allen: "Yes do that", over AnvilScore's curve, which gave x1.0). Supersedes "all strikes done = 100%" as the only finish.
- **The anvil starts from the ingot** (Allen: "make the anvil start from the ingot and shape into the blade shape"): the blank is an ingot-proportioned bar.
- **No strike hint, no % on Finish, no white gradient** (Allen: "its redundant and obvious").
- **A weather all the time, the better ones rarer** (Allen). Claude's defaults: back to back, 5 min each, never repeated: Sunshine 35, Snow 30, Rain 20, Rainbow 15. Snow speeds the mail-order clock x2 (not the sale letters); Rain +0.5 luck; Rainbow VIPs 15% (from 5%). No bonuses on a plot still in the tutorial; the look shows for everyone. Supersedes the free Sunshine every 15–25 min (2026-10-27).
- **VIP buyers glow gold** (Allen: "VIP NPCs should be highlighted"); not through walls.
- **No curtains at the shop entrance** (Allen: "remove these entrance curtains").
- **World labels keep a steady pixel size** (Allen: "the ores text look small sometimes"): ore labels 110×42 px within 16 studs; pad labels a thin dark-green stroke (Allen: "match the reference in thickness and color").

## Upgrade order built — 2026-10-28

Allen: "Build it". Built in Edit as decided in "Upgrade order" above, not played. Claude's defaults in the build: **Kenji after Mei, Rin after Kenji** (the walkthrough's "Kenji and Rin after Mei"); the Zanbatō and Big Sword Stand wait for the Cashier (the two-stage rule) instead of the Sword Etcher, and the Zanbatō costs more than the Etcher; Display Table 4 waits for the Falchion (was the Gladius, 2026-10-28); the crafters learn a new sword when its pad is bought, not after you've made one (supersedes Employees' "Training a shape is a one-time payment, after you have made that shape yourself" for every sword you know); Falchion and Gladius training cost 4,000 / 6,000, only for a shape the workshop lacks. Prices: Katana 1,300, Sword Crafter 10,000, Longsword 17,000, Haru 23,000, Stocker 36,000, Falchion 50,000, Mei 70,000, Shop Promoter 130,000, Gladius 150,000, Cashier 210,000, Sword Etcher 270,000, Zanbatō 300,000, Kenji 460,000, Rin 710,000. [[Sessions/2026-10-28#Upgrade order built]]

## Fixes3 — 2026-10-28

- **The first letter 3 s after the first sale** (Allen: "the mail quest should start say 3 seconds after the first part of the tutorial where the player just sells the sword"). Later letters keep 45 s.
- **Weather as a bottom-left icon** (Allen: "Instead of the weather being text ... have it on the bottom left like this", Create Pottery's). Claude's defaults: original icons in the pack's style (the pack has no sun, snow or rainbow); a tap shows the bonus and the time left.
- **Pad labels: the name, then the price with "$" underneath, the Buy button under both** (Allen: "the price should be underneath the description. and use $ before the price"; "the buy button should be underneath the price").

## More features — 2026-10-28

- **Gem Shop: Better Advertising, More VIPs, More Clients, Quicker Mail, five levels each** (Allen's [[More features]]: "Player should start at a base of 2 clients and be able to upgrade to around 7 clients max"; Quicker Mail "at a base of every 3 minutes ... 5 upgrades levels"; "more customers sohuld have 5 upgrades", "more vips should have 5 upgrades"). **More Clients is the mail's letters at once (2 → 7)**, not customers in the shop; customers at once stay 2 (+ Promoter, + Rickshaw Bays). Claude's defaults: Quicker Mail 3:00 → 1:15, More VIPs 5% → 15%, Better Advertising levels 3–5 at 25–45 / 20–38 / 16–30 s, and the prices of the new levels; the Rainbow triples the VIP chance instead of setting it to 15%. Supersedes 2026-10-22's "More Clients (40/100: 2 → 4 at once)" and the orders' random 3–10 minutes (Refinements3) and reputation's 2–3 slots.
- **Mail orders name a sword shape and a metal, sometimes an etch** (Allen: "specify a specific type of sword and metal used + etch(if unlocked and requested, should be a chance that it is)"). Claude's defaults: a metal you have collected, commoner ones likelier; etched 30% once the Etching Table is bought; no Polished or Rarity+ mail orders (Deliveries keep them).
- **No order right after the tutorial** (Allen: "as soon as i finish the mail tutorial - im getting another request"): the mail doesn't open full; the first order comes a Quicker Mail gap later.
- **Upgrade levels as ticks**, not "2/5" (Allen, after Create Pottery's bars). **Other players' pads hidden** on your screen. **Auto-Roll bar tweens in and out.** **Sounds:** the roll's spin and finish, a light rain loop. **Rain upright.** **Weather tip as plain text** naming the bonus ("2x Customers"). **"Sword Rack"** (was Finished Sword Rack). **Pad label outline 3 px.** [[Sessions/2026-10-28#More features]]
- **The hotbar matches the pickers** (Allen: "can we make our toolbar guis match the pickers"): charcoal cards with the hand-drawn edge, cream text, a white item rim, an amber edge on the stack in hand. Supersedes 2026-10-21's rarity-coloured slots with a halftone corner and black rims. **Shelves without their gold corners, studs, crest and gables** (Allen: "Remove the Stud and Corner and Crest and 2 Gables from all the shelves"). [[Sessions/2026-10-28#Hotbar like the pickers, plainer shelves]]

## Upgrades note — answers, 2026-10-28

Allen's [[Upgrades]] list, his answers before anything was built:

- **Plot size:** "Show me outlines first" — 2–3 sizes drawn on a top-down capture to pick from (as on 2026-10-26).
- **Blacksmith outside / one shared roof:** "Both, side by side" — two inert prototype copies to compare; the live plot untouched. The outside smithy is **fully open**: no cover, weather falls on everything.
- **Decorations:** **fixed decoration spots**, bought with **coins**; the first set **lanterns and lights, plants and pots, banners and signs** (no trophies yet). Purely cosmetic (GDD §10).
- **Shop interior walls:** one colour, **warm plaster cream**, no dark timber inside; the outside unchanged.
- **Floating shelf:** only the 3 boards, **keeping the cradles** the swords sit in.

## Anvil stations, Your anvil, Furnace 3 - 2026-10-28

- **Each anvil is a station with props** (Allen): a quench tub and a hammer stump (hand hammer, tongs, a sledgehammer) behind each anvil. Claude's choice of props. They wait for the outside smithy: the indoor workshop has no room behind the anvils.
- **"Your anvil" overhead text** over Anvil 1, seen only by its owner (Allen: "just an overhead text that says 'Your anvil'"). Crew anvils stay usable by you when their crafter is away (Allen: "Keep as today").
- **Furnace 3** (Allen: "Just Furnace 3"), 5,800 after Anvil 3; **Haru waits for it** (Claude's default, keeping the Anvil 2 rule that a working crafter has its own furnace).
- **Stations in working order round the yard** (Allen: "somewhat linear and make sense while being organized and also looking cohesive", after Create Pottery's edge layout): the Line prototype. [[Sessions/2026-10-28#Anvil stations, Your anvil, Furnace 3, the Line layout]]
- **The Hilt Bench is folded into the anvil** (Allen: "Fold it in", then "if they hit finish early, then it goes to the hilting process"): the server fits the hilt when the blade is saved, and the anvil plays the drag-on seat right after, from a full forge or an early Finish. No carried bare blades; no backing out of the seat. The bench stays as decor for now. [[Sessions/2026-10-28#Hilt Bench folded into the anvil]]
- **The blacksmith area is outside, for real** (Allen: "Build it for real"): the Line layout on the plot template, on paving in the workshop floor's tile with a lawn round it (Allen: "have the ground underneath the stations be our stylized tile ... and then grass surrounding it"). The Upgraded Store now pushes only what's in the strip behind the shop. Supersedes the indoor workshop (2026-10-20 onward) and 2026-10-21's push of the whole blacksmith area. [[Sessions/2026-10-28#The outside smithy, built]]
- **Smaller anvils on square wooden blocks** (Allen: "have the anvil smaller and fit on square wooden base"): all four at 0.7, the working face at the same height, on a 2.4-stud timber block. Size is Claude's default. [[Sessions/2026-10-28#Smaller anvils on wooden blocks]]
- **The Hilt Bench is removed** (Allen: "Remove it"), and carried bare blades with it: every blade leaves the anvil as a finished sword. The bench's end of the row is open floor. [[Sessions/2026-10-28#Hilt Bench removed, carried blades gone]]
- **Wall racks at shoulder height; cradles appear only under a sword** (Allen): the bottom shelf at about shoulder height (raised 2.75, Claude's measure for the default R15); a holder's cradles are hidden until a sword goes on, then build in like a bought item and the sword pops (1.2x, 0.3 s; Claude's numbers). Display tables' cradles too. [[Sessions/2026-10-29#Wall racks raised, cradles build in, sword pop]]
- **Shop doors and the delivery bay** (Allen): the back doorway moves one bay away from the delivery side; the shop-to-bay doorway is walled up (supersedes the 2026-10-23 door "teased from the start"); the bay's own fence goes (the plot fence bounds it); table stand bases hide until a sword is on them. [[Sessions/2026-10-29#Back door moved, delivery doorway walled up, bay fence gone, table bases hidden]]
- **Station signs on standing frames; fuller frames; anvil hearths** (Allen): the floating signs and wall props of the Polishing Station and Etching Tables stand on timber frames (posts, beam, backboard) [[Sessions/2026-10-29#Standing frames for the station signs (trial)]]; the polishing frame shows four polished swords and each etching frame six etched ones; Anvils 2-4 stand on slate hearths like Anvil 1's instead of the round stone. [[Sessions/2026-10-29#More swords on the station frames; hearths for Anvils 2-4]]

## Ten new swords - 2026-10-29

- **Ten more sword types** (Allen: "need at least 7 more sword types", then named them): Machete, Scimitar, Cutlass, Jian, Khopesh, Dadao, Rapier, Hook Sword, Claymore, Flamberge, each from researched proportions, slightly exaggerated, same 12-strike recipe. [[Sessions/2026-10-29#Ten new swords]]
- **Claymore and Flamberge are big swords** (Claude's call): at real length they are about 7 studs with the hilt, past the Zanbatō, so only the Big Sword Stand takes them and they sell above it (1,400 and 1,900). The Rapier stays ordinary at 96 cm (real ~104) so it fits the wall racks.
- **A second blueprint pad** (Claude's proposal, prices not run through the economy model): beside the first, a chain Machete 600 → Scimitar 6,000 → Cutlass 26,000 → Jian 80,000 → Khopesh 190,000 → Dadao 240,000 → Rapier 380,000 → Hook Sword 520,000 → Claymore 700,000 → Flamberge 950,000, each after a spine goal of its band (Wakizashi, Katana, Longsword, Falchion, Gladius, Cashier, Zanbatō), so the sword spine never waits for them. Values sit between the spine swords around them.
- **Every new sword has its own hilt** (Allen: "if the swords are known for their hilts, we should build custom hilts for them in Blender"): Machete (slab grip, rivets, butt hook), Scimitar (pistol grip, down-turned quillons), Cutlass (cupped shell, D knuckle bow), Jian (ears toward the blade, shaped pommel), Khopesh (no guard, fan pommel), Dao (disc guard, ring pommel), Rapier (swept hilt), Hook Sword (crescent guard, dagger pommel), Claymore (forward quillons, quatrefoils), Flamberge (wide cross, side rings, parrying lugs). The Zanbatō stays on the katana hilt (Claude's call: its tier tsubas live on that template).
- **BladeData is edited in Studio** from now on: the vault's `Prototypes/tanto-forge` JSON and exporter are stale (since the 2026-10-28 blank).
- **Seven fictional swords next** (Allen picked from [[References/Sword Silhouettes/fictional-silhouettes-v1.png]]): Crescent Fang, Shard Blade, Serpent, Leviathan Cleaver, Seraph, Thornspire, Moonbite, looking like their references, each with an ornate custom hilt. Sakabatō, Ember Tongue (read as katanas), Twin Fang, Moonhook and Sawtooth (need new blade-mesh work) wait. [[Design/Sword Profile Ideas#Fictional]]

## Seven fictional swords - 2026-10-29

- **Seven fictional swords built** (Allen's picks from [[References/Sword Silhouettes/fictional-silhouettes-v1.png]]: Crescent Fang, Shard Blade, Moonbite, Serpent, Leviathan Cleaver, Thornspire, Seraph), each with its own ornate Blender hilt. **Seraph and Thornspire copy References/Swords/roblox unique swords.png** (Allen: "copy the exact designs"): traced from the straightened picture, silhouette IoU 0.94 and 0.93. **Moonbite keeps only the silhouette idea** of its Demon Slayer reference. [[Sessions/2026-10-29#Seven fictional swords]]
- **The Thornspire's open slot is painted on** (Claude's call: a BladeCore blade has one outline per station and can't have a hole), and its bottom horns are flanks rather than overhanging hooks.
- **None of the seven is big** (Claude's call, the 7-stud rule): the Leviathan Cleaver is 5.9 studs with its hilt, the Seraph 6.1.
- **A third blueprint pad** beside the second, a chain after the Flamberge: Crescent Fang 1,200,000 → Shard Blade 1,500,000 → Moonbite 1,850,000 → Serpent 2,300,000 → Leviathan Cleaver 2,800,000 → Thornspire 3,400,000 → Seraph 4,200,000; values 480 to 980, above every ordinary real sword but under the Zanbatō, so a crafted sword never out-earns the one-per-stand big swords (Claude's proposal; the economy model puts them at 16-28 h for a casual player).
- **Hilt parts can keep their own colour at every tier** (`KeepLook`): gems, the Seraph's white wings, the Thornspire's gold bands and both swords' blade markings; Grip, Guard and Pommel still follow the tier.

## Hilt quality pass - 2026-10-29

Allen: "make sure the blender models look perfect, like the references i gave you". [[Sessions/2026-10-29#Hilt quality pass]]
- **Eleven real-sword hilts rebuilt** against the silhouette sheet and measured real swords (cutlass half-basket, swept rapier, khopesh fan pommel, shamshir bird's head, flamberge leather ricasso and flame lugs, claymore quatrefoils, wheel pommels on longsword and falchion, bigger gladius pommel, shorter hook-sword spike, oval dao guard); Jian and Machete kept. Every hilt ≤ 3,500 triangles and passes `qa_check --profile roblox`.
- **Real form wins over the sheet where they disagree** (Claude's call): the claymore's quillons slope toward the blade (the sheet draws them toward the pommel), the scimitar keeps a bird's-head beak, the rapier's bow runs into its pommel. Front IoU against the sheet is 0.59-0.87, none at 0.9.
- **Normal swords' guards stay ≤ 1.36 studs** (Claude's call, the display tables' 1.4 slot spacing): the longsword's cross is 1.36, not the sheet's 1.67. Big swords' crosses 1.8 (Claymore, Flamberge). Open for Allen.
- **Grip lengths and blade offsets unchanged**; `Back` follows the new pommels (swords up to 0.1 stud longer, the hook sword 0.17 shorter).

- **2026-10-29: Etch motifs follow a smoothed face** (Allen: the fictional etches were "uneven and asymmetrical"). Outline features (thorns, wings, steps, the bite, flame waves) no longer move or resize motifs, and the etch runs to the real tip. This applies to every sword; regular ones barely change.
- **2026-10-29: Traced fictional parts are mirrored from one half** (Allen: "do one half and reflect the other half over"). Thornspire's marks reflect about the blade's own middle; every Thornspire and Seraph hilt part reflects about the centre plane.

- **2026-10-29: Blueprints aren't gated by the crafter anvils** (Allen: "the price alone is already enough"). The Falchion and Gladius no longer wait for Anvil 3 / Anvil 4.

- **2026-10-29: Three blueprint pads by group** (Allen picked East / West+World / Fictional). Eastern holds the Japanese and Chinese swords, Western & World holds the European swords plus the Machete, Scimitar and Khopesh, and Fictional holds the seven fictional swords. Each pad is in price order; the only gates kept are physical (the Big Sword Stand, plus the Zanbatō's Cashier).

- **2026-10-29: Big swords sell only on the Big Sword Stand** (Allen: "both", [[Design/Economy Review — 2026-10-29]]). The cart refuses them and makes no orders for them; carted at 0.8×, a Flamberge out-earned every sword sold in the shop.
- **2026-10-29: The fictional pad opens after the Hook Sword, priced 0.7M–2.4M** (was 1.2M–4.2M after the Flamberge). Each costs 25–41 minutes of a casual player's income, and the Seraph comes at about 20 h 30.

- **2026-10-29: Mail orders ask for your best swords and pay like a sale** (Allen). They pick from your top three regular swords (3:2:1), and the metal counts at no less than a typical ingot at your luck.

- **2026-10-29: Gameplay simulator: a Python discrete-event simulator, with Studio as the only source of truth** ([[Development/Gameplay Simulator Plan]]). An exporter writes `balance.json` and golden test vectors from the real Luau, and pytest checks the Python rules against them. Allen's answers:
  - Focus on the first hour and the full curve equally.
  - Count played time only.
  - Use estimated hand-action timings for now.
  - Include paid items in v1 as a switch on any archetype.

- **2026-10-29: Simulator settings** ([[Development/Gameplay Simulator Plan]] §8):
  - **Targets:** Economy Pass 2's targets extended to the Seraph at about 20 h.
  - **Forge quality:** about 100% for everyone (an estimate).
  - **Payers:** free, Starter Pack and all passes, compared.
  - **Stuck:** 10 min.
  - **Reports:** a vault note plus HTML and CSV.
  - **Logistics:** modelled (crafters stall on an empty or full rack).
  - **Archetypes:** six: Casual, Efficient, Automator, Rusher, Grinder and Manager (keeps every rack, shelf, cart and mail full).

- **2026-10-29: Customers come to an empty shop** (Allen: "customers should spawn and come in all the time, even if the store is empty"). They look over a holder, say there's nothing for sale and leave; they still take a place in the shop while there.

- **2026-10-29: Simulator hand times come from Allen's playtests.** A Studio-only `PlaytestLog` records his actions. `calibrate.py` replaces an estimate once it has 5 or more samples. Actions the server can't see stay estimates. The full loop, from ore to shelf to sale, is a reported metric (Allen: "another important metric is doing the whole loop").

- **2026-10-29: No walk-ins to an empty shop before the first sale** (Allen: "No walk-ins to an empty shop until the tutorial's first sword is on display"). The tutorial stays as it was until then.

- **2026-10-29: The simulator plays 1 h a day.** Allen: "1 h a day". Each day brings a daily reward; the cart and mail catch up in between.
- **2026-10-29: The simulator's gem buys differ by player type.** Allen: "Differs by type".
  - Manager and Grinder buy mail upgrades first.
  - Efficient and Rusher buy customer upgrades first.
  - Casual buys the cheapest next level.

- **2026-10-29: New progression targets.** Allen: "the katana target ideally should be 15 minutes ... sword crafter should take about 1h 30 minutes, long sword could come at 40 minutes". The Longsword now comes before the Crafter. Falchion 4 h, Gladius 6 h 20, Zanbatō 8 h and Seraph 20 h are unchanged. They are the simulator's scoring targets; the game's prices aren't changed.

- **2026-10-29: Targets for every major milestone.** Allen: "Use the proposed targets as they are". These are casual-player hours of play:
  - 1st / 2nd / 3rd merge: 2:00 / 4:00 / 7:00
  - Haru: 3:00
  - Every gem upgrade at Lv. 1: 3:00
  - Stocker: 3:30
  - Mei: 6:00
  - Shop Promoter: 6:40
  - Cashier: 7:30
  - Sword Etcher: 8:30
  - The type's gem pair at Lv. 3: 10:00
  - Kenji: 10:30
  - Rin: 12:30
  - A gem pair at Lv. 5: 20:00

  These are in addition to the Katana 0:15, Longsword 0:40, Crafter 1:30, Falchion 4:00, Gladius 6:20, Zanbatō 8:00 and Seraph 20:00. They score the simulator; the game's prices aren't changed.

- **2026-10-29: Special Orders: a 5-minute skip wait, and they pay only their order's gems.** Allen: "okay then do that".
  - A skipped cart offer's slot stays empty for `Deliveries.SKIP_WAIT` (300 s), showing a clock and a countdown, then a new offer comes.
  - Accepting still refills at once.
  - A Special Order pays its order's gems (1–3). It no longer adds one per sword after the first; Bulk is unchanged.
  - Why: instant free skips plus per-sword gems let a player keep the cart running for about 1,500 gems a day.

## 2026-10-29: Cashier and Shop Promoter cheaper and apart

Allen: "cashier comes after shop promoter, both are way too expensive". Cashier 50,000 after the Upgraded Store and the Stocker; Shop Promoter 40,000 after Display Table 1 and the Longsword (were 210,000 after the Promoter and 130,000 after the Falchion). Prices Claude's, not modelled. Supersedes their places in the goal order above. [[Sessions/2026-10-29]]

## 2026-10-29: Cheap packs, Starter Pack coins, both etching tables, a second polishing station

- **9 and 19 R$ packs, coins and gems** (Allen: "need more entry level purchases, 9 robux purchases 19 robux purchases"; asked which, "both"): 800 / 1,800 coins and 7 / 15 gems. Amounts Claude's.
- **Starter Pack: 100 gems and 10,000 coins** (Allen: "should give a set amount of gold not just a gold ingot"; asked, "Coins only"). Supersedes the Gold ingot in [[Design/Monetization Plan]] §2.
- **Both Etching Tables are the player's** (asked; "Yes, both tables usable"); the Sword Etcher keeps working at Table 2.
- **Polishing Station 2**, 2,500 (Allen: "adding another polishing station to buy makes sense"; price Claude's).
[[Sessions/2026-10-29]]

- **2026-10-29: The Longsword path at 60%.** Allen: "i think we should lower the costs for now" (no pinned goal for now). Machete 600 → 360, Scimitar 6,000 → 3,600, Longsword 17,000 → 10,200, in `Shared.Upgrades`.
  - Simulated (free, 8 h): Efficient's Longsword 1:09 → 0:32 and Manager's 1:06 → 0:31, both on the 40-minute target.
  - Casual's moves only 2:37 → 1:54: it still buys 38 cheaper things first. A pinned next goal is what moves Casual.

- **2026-10-29: Cheaper late swords** (Allen: "Do B", then "Yes, both"). Each sword after the Gladius now costs about 25 minutes of a good player's income when it's bought; they cost 29–72 minutes, so the end game was a 20–40 minute save per goal.

  | Sword | Was | Now |

  | Dadao | 240k | 170k |
  | Zanbatō | 300k | 250k |

  Simulated: after hour 16, a quarter → two-thirds of 10-minute stretches bring a new upgrade, and the game is about 30% shorter (a good free player owns every pad in about 16 h instead of 23).

Cart Special Orders keep theirs. Simulated: +200–700 gems over the whole game for players who work orders. No type fills the gem shop before the end either way.

## 2026-10-29: Decorate camera mode

- **Decorate is a camera mode with a bar along the bottom** (Allen: "it'd be nice if it toggles you into a camera/edit mode, where you can click on a section and the camera pans ... i can buy decorations from the selector down below. just an inspiration"): you stand still, section chips (Shop, Outside, later Workshop) move the camera to frame that section, cards show its decorations with prices or how many you have, pick a card and tap a + to place (buying it if none are left), tap a placed one's bag to take it back. Replaces walking round with + markers. Claude's calls: one level of sections (no sub-chips), the view angles, coins shown in the bar, the pick-then-place flow, X / B / Backspace to leave. [[Sessions/2026-10-29#Decorate camera mode]]

## 2026-10-29: Features and fixes.md

- **The crew window is one scrolling page with a section per role, titled Crew** (Allen's words in [[Sessions/2026-10-29#Features and fixes.md]]); the left tabs are gone.
- **Crafters can make Random:** each sword a random trained shape (`making = "random"`, saved); only the active shape is marked.
- **Buy buttons are never greyed for want of coins or gems:** a tap offers the pack. Veils stay for gates ("After ..."). Supersedes "An upgrade you can't afford is veiled" (GemController, 2026-10-23).
- **Pill toggles get a black outline** (Allen asked). Supersedes "no borders on pills" for toggles; other buttons keep no border.
- **Phones show prompts as a bare tap square** (no words); desktop keeps the words, and the Etching / Polishing prompts name their boost.
- **Window text is at least 23 pt (16 px on a phone at 0.7)**; chips 20.

## 2026-10-29: Analytics funnels

- **One event vocabulary for playtests and live players:** the services' `PlaytestLog.log` events also feed `ServerScriptService.Analytics` in every server, so Studio's calibration log and the Creator Dashboard measure the same things ([[Development/Analytics Funnels]]).
- **The progression funnel is minutes played (1, 5, 10, 15, 30, 60, 120, 300, 600), not an upgrade order.** Players buy pads in different orders and Roblox fills in skipped funnel steps, so an upgrade-ordered funnel would report false completions. Each pad, crew hire, merge, gem level and letter is a Milestone event instead, with the minutes played and the simulator report's time bin.
- **Onboarding steps after the tutorial's end (first upgrade, Gem Shop, gem buy) that happen before it are logged when it ends,** so an early one can't mark the tutorial done. "Bought Ore Slot 2" became "Bought an upgrade" (any pad), since the guide no longer asks for Ore Slot 2.
- **2026-10-29: Book sword cards: dark card, 12 amber rays, a softer warm glow and a sword drop shadow** (Allen picked from renders); the halftone fills the whole card on both pages ([[Sessions/2026-10-29#Book sword cards pop]]).
- **2026-10-29: Ore rolls escalate with rarity** (Allen picked 1, 2 and 3 of five ideas): bigger pops, sparks from Rare, camera shake from Epic, a light column for Legendary/Secret, a weighted landing, and a rarity callout from Rare. No near-miss effects (as before) ([[Sessions/2026-10-29#Ore rolls more satisfying]]).
- **2026-10-30: Textured surroundings, smooth stations** (Allen agreed to try Claude's split, then picked charcoal): ground, walls, roofs and fence keep their textures, and the things players use are SmoothPlastic in flat colours. The yard paving is a softer version of option 1 (`PaintFloor1Soft`), the anvils, forges and ingot racks are SmoothPlastic, and the anvil hearths are charcoal (58, 54, 54) ([[Sessions/2026-10-30#Yard paving: six FreeStylized options]]).
- **2026-10-30: The hilt seat hits harder and reveals the sword** (Allen picked 1, 2 and 4 of four ideas: the reveal, a harder seat, haptics; he left out a drag with resistance and a magnet snap). The drag is still untimed and can't fail. Effects grow with the metal's rarity. The reveal's sweep is in the rarity colour, not the white polish glare, so a polished blade still looks different ([[Sessions/2026-10-30#Hilting more satisfying]]).
- **2026-10-30: Faster Strikes I–V speed up your strikes; the 12 strikes and their stages stay** (Allen: "call it Faster Strikes upgrade"; he turned down hold-to-strike, since the cooldown sets the pace, and fewer heavier strikes, which "would take away the the stages"). Strike cycle ×1, 0.9 … 0.5 (0.75 s → ~0.375 s); the dent's own time isn't scaled. Prices Claude's: 250 / 1,000 / 2,500 / 5,000 / 10,000, gated on the Wakizashi, Katana, Sword Crafter, Anvil 3 and Falchion, priced to pay for themselves. Server floor 0.35 → 0.25 s a strike. [[Sessions/2026-10-30#Faster Strikes]]
- **2026-10-30: A delivery step's tick is a badge on its icon, and each trip chip is as wide as its longest text** (Allen: "the checkmark covers some text"); the Mail window gets a fixed width like the other windows. [[Sessions/2026-10-30#Mail window width; delivery trip ticks]]
- **2026-10-30: The Deliveries window is layout C** (Allen: "the layout seems a bit confusing", then "Use C" of three built options): a strip for the order you're on and the cart's four steps, and under it your swords while you pack, else the offers two across, or the cart upgrades behind Upgrade cart. Offers aren't shown while packing (Allen: "whats the point in that?": Accept is locked then). No tabs, no separate packing page, no trip row, no help card. Supersedes 2026-10-22's "Orders and Cart tabs ... the four-step trip tracker" layout (the rules are unchanged). [[Sessions/2026-10-30#Deliveries layout C]]
- **2026-10-30: Prompts are the game's own square** (Allen: "only the touch icon square. the text can go below it ... with the handdrawn borders"): a hand-drawn-edged square with the tap icon or key, the action under it, on every device (Allen: "Square everywhere"). Supersedes 2026-10-29's "Phones show prompts as a bare tap square (no words)". [[Sessions/2026-10-30#Refinements (2026-10-30)]]
- **2026-10-30: Finishes show the value going up** (Allen: "make it satisfying for the player to see the value of the sword going up"): pickers' chips read now → after, and the etch and polish views count the sword's value up as the finish lands (a pill in the view, not a popup). [[Sessions/2026-10-30#Refinements (2026-10-30)]]
- **2026-10-30: HUD coin and gem numbers are two layers** (Allen: "two texts layered on top of each other ... another all black text underneath"): a white outlined number over a black copy a little lower. [[Sessions/2026-10-30#Refinements (2026-10-30)]]
- **2026-10-30: HUD menu is 2 × 2: Collection, Upgrades, Shop, Crew; HUD labels in capitals with a thick dark outline** (Allen). [[Sessions/2026-10-30#Refinements (2026-10-30)]]
- **2026-10-30: Overhead and HUD text is two layers everywhere** (Allen: "apply our text change ... to all the overheads in the game like the employees"): the coloured text with its black edge over a black copy a little lower; dark pills and speech bubbles keep one layer. **The delivery strip puts its actions under the steps and its status on the right** (Allen's words in [[Sessions/2026-10-30#Refinements, second round (Allen's playtest notes)]]); crates are tiles above your swords, the strip has a load bar.
- **2026-10-30: No polish messages at all** (Allen: "why am i still seeing this, thought we removed it"): the partial save's "Polish saved (n%)" toast goes too; only errors still show. The polish value counts up toward each new value as you polish. [[Sessions/2026-10-30#Polish value climbs as you polish]]
- **2026-10-30: Polishing and etching send coins into the station's value pill** (Allen: "the coins spawn on the screen for a bit and then tween to the coins bar, and then the counter goes up"): the pill in the station's view, since the coins are the sword's value going up, not your balance. [[Sessions/2026-10-30#Coins fly into the value as you polish and etch]]
- **2026-10-30: Hotbar slots are a metal sheen in their item's rarity colour** (Allen picked the bright rarity sheen from flat colours, gradients and sheens in other metals). Supersedes 2026-10-28's charcoal hotbar cards. [[Art/Refinements 2026-10-30/hotbar-rarity-sheen.png]]
- **2026-10-30: Faster Strikes II-V just chain** (Allen: "Just chain them"): each after the level before, no milestone gates; a casual picker has Lv. 5 at about 1 h 40. Supersedes this morning's gates on the Katana, Sword Crafter, Anvil 3 and Falchion. [[Sessions/2026-10-30#Hammer upright; Faster Strikes chained]]
- **2026-10-30: Buying an upgrade bursts at its pad, and a stat upgrade's station reacts** (Allen picked these over a trail to the station and over furnace models that grow or swap): confetti and a gold ring at the pad with a chime; sparks, a flash and "Heat 10s → 6s" / "Strikes 20% faster" at the furnace or anvil. [[Sessions/2026-10-30#Upgrade purchase effects]]
- **2026-10-30: Polish Boost I-III raises the polish bonus to 13 / 16 / 20% on every polished sword you own** (Allen: "making the polishing station also upgradable? mainly just increasing the price boost %", "yes all polished swords"). 2,000 / 8,000 / 25,000, chained after the Polishing Station. [[Sessions/2026-10-30#Polish Boost]]
- **2026-10-30: Two kinds of customer, regular and VIP; a decline's raise chance halves each time** (Allen: "can't we just have 2 types of customers regular and vip ... 50% and then half it every time"; "regular range from 1.15 to 1.50. VIP from 2 - 3.5x"). Regulars offer 1.15-1.5x the value and raise at 50%, then 25%, 12.5% ...; VIPs offer 2-3.5x with no haggling. No favourite swords. Supersedes the Farmer / Adventurer / Knight (2026-10-17) and "value to +20%" (2026-10-24); VIP was 1.75-2.25 (2026-10-27). Casual Zanbato 8 h 17 -> 7 h 15. [[Sessions/2026-10-30#Two kinds of customer; the mail popup times out]]
- **2026-10-30: Every mail popup times out with a countdown** (Allen: "the message is not going away", "add a small countdown on the top right of it"): 8 s for an order, 15 s for a story letter, which then waits in the mail without popping again that session. [[Sessions/2026-10-30#Two kinds of customer; the mail popup times out]]
- **2026-10-30: The Big Sword Stand and the Zanbatō no longer wait for the Cashier** (Allen: "We should decouple big sword stand from cashier"). The stand comes after the Upgraded Store, the Zanbatō after the Dadao and the stand. Supersedes the two-stage default of 2026-10-28. Part of moving the Zanbatō target to about 4 h (Allen: "make the zanbato target of say 4 hours instead of 8"); its prices are still proposed. [[Sessions/2026-10-30#The Zanbatō at 4 h and the first employee as a milestone]]
- **2026-10-30: The Zanbatō target is about 4 h, and the first employee is a milestone** (Allen: "make the zanbato target of say 4 hours instead of 8. Seems like a lot just to be the fifth upgraded sword"; "It should feel like a milestone to finally have your first employee"; "the upgrades should not be necessarily too easy to get either"; then "Yes" to the prices). Jian 28,000, Dadao 60,000, Zanbatō 88,000 (were 80,000 / 170,000 / 250,000); Sword Crafter 40,000 (was 10,000); ×2 for the racks, heat, stations, display holders, Faster Strikes, Polish Boost and the cart pads: Ingot Rack 460, Ingot Rack 2 2,000, Sword Rack 280, Sword Rack 2 2,600, Faster Heating I–III 580 / 3,000 / 5,200, Furnace 2 2,800 (its heating 1,800 / 9,000 / 15,600), Furnace 3 11,600 (7,000 / 36,000 / 62,000), Faster Strikes I–V 500 / 2,000 / 5,000 / 10,000 / 20,000, Polishing Station 200, Polishing Station 2 5,000, Polish Boost I–III 4,000 / 16,000 / 50,000, Etching Table 1,580, Etching Table 2 240,000, Wall Sword Rack 2 106, Back Wall Rack 900, Wall Sword Racks 3–4 6,400 / 30,000, Display Tables 1–6 440 / 9,000 / 42,000 / 46,000 / 80,000 / 120,000, Upgraded Store 6,000, Delivery Cart 6,200, Rickshaw Bays 1–3 2,200 / 44,000 / 152,000. Unchanged: the tutorial's, the ore slots, the other swords, the crew past the crafter, the Cashier, Shop Promoter and Big Sword Stand. Supersedes the 8 h Zanbatō of 2026-10-27/29. Simulated: the Zanbatō at 2:34–4:31 and the crafter at 1:40–2:16 for efficient, manager and grinder players. [[Sessions/2026-10-30#New prices: the Zanbatō at 4 h, the crafter a milestone]]

[[Sessions/2026-10-30#One sword chain; the sim's main point is the Sword Crafter]]
